## Summary

The Debug Overlay (True Values) is a developer-only, display-only diagnostic layer that shows the authoritative value beside the perceived value for any ref and any player, names the difference (`HONEST`, `DELIBERATE` lie, `RETURNING`, or a named `DEFECT_*` class), and surfaces the kernel's own diagnostics (`state_hash`, dirty keys against `MAX_DIRTY_KEYS` 256, hook timings, the Graph's budget, Attention level, sanity per player) so that deliberate corruption can be told from real desync. It is excluded from every release build at the file level, is never an accessibility feature (art bible R-7), writes nothing, and owns the build gate, the channel registration contract, the seven MVP pages that the other systems' diagnostics plug into and the non-screen observer log *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. Read it if you design or test anything that lies to the player, or any system that publishes a diagnostics counter.

> **Quick reference** — Layer: `Polish` (the systems index and design order place it last because it reads everything; the GDD template's layer list has no Polish value) · Priority: `MVP` · Key deps: `Command & Simulation Layer (hard; designed)`, `Perception Filter (hard; designed in this run)`, `Pixel-Perfect Render Pipeline & Camera (soft; designed)`; it also reads, as owners' published views or channels, the Life-Support Graph, Suit & Equipment, Sanity, Attention, Horror, Emitter, Lighting, Layout, Body, Assembly, Save/Load, Web Map, Interaction, Controller, Boarding, Audio and Chart Table (all designed), plus In-World Instruments (designed (this run); IW-38, P-2) and the HUD & Suit Layer (designed; its nine `hud_debug_snapshot()` counters, P-1); Accessibility, Settings, Onboarding, Co-op Session and the dev console are undesigned (provisional contracts P-3 to P-12)

## Overview

The Debug Overlay (True Values) is a **developer-only, read-only diagnostic layer**: exactly one kernel subscriber of class `DIAGNOSTIC` plus a dev-build presentation layer at pipeline layer 4, which shows, for any chosen reference and any chosen player, the authoritative value (`true_value`) beside the perceived value (`perceived`) with a named classification of the difference, and next to them the kernel's own diagnostics: `T`, kernel state, queue depth, the last-N command log, the resolved hook order with per-hook timings, `state_hash`, dirty-key counts against `MAX_DIRTY_KEYS` 256, the Graph's hook budget, the Attention level and each player's sanity. Its single job is the sentence in the systems index (idx:43): to **tell deliberate corruption from real desync**. A hallucination is a lie that Sanity issued on purpose, that the Perception Filter rendered, and that carries a leak (Pillar 4: no lie is unfalsifiable). A defect is any other difference between what is true and what is shown: a stale value, a node that bypassed the Filter (a bypass is detected only by the render probe, DO-22), a lie with no leak, a determinism break *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*. To the player the two look identical, which is exactly why a tool that separates them is needed. The index says "authoritative values beside the *hallucinated* ones"; this GDD labels the pair **true** and **perceived**, because `Perceived` is the Filter's locked record name and an honest reading is also "perceived" (pf:247, PF-7: with no entry for the ref, `displayed` equals the true value). "Hallucinated" is index prose, not a label (orchestrator ruling: terminology reconciled, no edit to the index).

**How the player meets it: never.** The Overlay is absent from every **publicly released** build; it is present only in the supervised playtest preset (DO-6, D97), which is never handed to a player outside a measured session *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. It is excluded from public release exports at the file level, not switched off at runtime (DO-2), it is not a setting, not an accessibility option, not a rebinding entry and not a tutorial line (art bible R-7, ab:886: "An honest overlay on top of a lying HUD would make Pillar 4 switchable off, and the pillar is the game"). The player meets what the tool *protects*: the promise that when a gauge and the frost beside it disagree, the frost is the ship. This is a data and infrastructure layer with a pure player-side consequence, the same register as the kernel (kern:30: "the player never meets this system; they meet a promise it keeps").

**What the game loses without it.** (1) The Perception Filter's top technical risk (idx:281: "Per-client divergence is indistinguishable from desync without tooling"; mitigation "Debug Overlay (true values) built with it"): without the tool, every "the gauge lied and I could not tell" report is unclassifiable and Pillar 1's design test cannot be run. (2) The Sanity system's QA instrument: AC-SN-88 (stage-1 entry within 268 to 363 s of boarding, sn:1626) and AC-SN-144 (named blocker "deferred: Debug Overlay", sn:1678) can only be read off this layer, and Sanity's `eligible_entries_per_min` telemetry (sn:811) replaces a reference rate with a measured one only here. (3) The MVP kill/continue playtest gate: Boarding's X-BS-11 and AC-BS-122 require an observer to read Attention `phase` "from the debug overlay and never shown to the tester" (boarding-and-salvage.md:1187, :1498), so the gate cannot run without it. The observer reads through the non-screen **observer event log** (DO-35), never through anything the tester's screen can show. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* (4) Six already-written acceptance criteria that name it (kern:915, kern:916, AC-PF-40, AC-PF-79, AC-AT-66, pipe:1028) are unrunnable.

**What it owns, and what it deliberately does not.**

| The Overlay owns | The Overlay does not own (and must not absorb) |
|---|---|
| The shared **build gate** and **dev source root** that make "absent from release" a file-list fact (DO-2) | Any simulation state: no block, hook, mutator, command, read view or RNG stream (DO-1, DO-9) |
| The one `DIAGNOSTIC` subscription and the model/view split (DO-3, DO-4) | The kernel's diagnostics accessor shape, timer and counters (the kernel's; requested in DO-33) |
| The **channel registration contract** and the naming and severity convention that closes the three sibling "freeze the `diag.*` / `PC_*` / `IA_*` names" asks (DO-12, DO-13) | Any sibling's diagnostic names, counters or panel content (owners register them; the Overlay restates none as a fact) |
| The seven MVP pages as data tables and the generic `P-CHANNELS` page (DO-15) | A per-system panel design for Horror, Body, Lighting, Emitter, Layout, Save/Load, Interaction, Controller, Web Map, Audio, Assembly or Boarding |
| The **discriminator** `desync_classifier` (DO-21) | The Filter's leak math, amplitudes, tick phases or rendering (the Overlay calls `true_value` and `perceived` and displays PF-41's counters) |
| The toggle path, the four modes and the observer-visibility rules (DO-6 to DO-8) | The dev console, `force_stage` / `force_level`, Suit forcing, runtime-knob changes (owners' debug APIs behind the same gate; the Overlay is display only, DO-1) |
| The text `dump` for retained QA evidence (DO-31), the non-screen observer event log and the defect ring (DO-35) | The diagnostics pipeline, CSV or JSON export, hash algorithm (H64) or the K-1 per-block hash (ADR candidates) |

**Tier and size.** Category Meta, layer Polish by dependency (it reads everything), priority **MVP** (index row 26; TD-FEASIBILITY: "needed from the start"). The concept's scope table lists the true-value overlay under Alpha (con:495); the index, written later, and every sibling GDD treat it as MVP, and they win (a concept-document staleness, recorded in Open Questions, not a design conflict). Estimated effort **L** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: re-estimated from S; the cost is real and is spread across sibling epics. The systems index row still reads S, a `cross_gdd_note` for the index owner)*. The GDD is a full eight-section document (the producer's "thin technical spec" recommendation for infrastructure systems was declined, idx:143), and stays proportionate by **breadth**: one registered-channel row per sibling, seven pages as data tables, no per-system panel design, no console, no export pipeline. **Epic split for `/create-epics` (four epics):** (E1) the gate and composition root (DO-2, the playtest preset of DO-6, the input path of DO-8, **the observer event log and defect ring, DO-35**); (E2) the model and the kernel-facing pages (DO-3, DO-4, DO-15 to DO-19, DO-23 to DO-25, DO-32 to DO-34; formulas F-2 to F-9); (E3) the classifier and its seams to the Filter, Sanity and Instruments (DO-20 to DO-22, formula F-1; the published `lie_targets` and intent records are a pre-epic dependency, OQ-DO-26). **The playtest-gate critical path is E1 plus DO-35 and the export carve-out; it does not wait on E3.** Slip candidates, in order: F-8 (an advisory copy), `P-FRAME`, F-4 and F-5 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; (E4) the view, the channel contract, the world draws and the evidence (DO-5, DO-12 to DO-14, DO-26 to DO-28, DO-31).

**MVP scope (one statement for review and `/create-epics`).** *MVP:* the build gate and the CI file-list check (DO-2); the one `DIAGNOSTIC` subscriber (DO-3); the four modes and the playtest preset (DO-6); the observer event log and defect ring (DO-35); the seven pages plus the generic `P-CHANNELS` page (DO-15); the `desync_classifier` for class (b) refs (false system states, `CLASSES_ENABLED` {b}) on the local seat (DO-21), where **E3 degrades to the fallbacks of E-DO-49** (`UNVERIFIED`, `SUBJECT_SCOPE`, `NO_RESOLVER`, the coverage readout) until the OQ-DO-26 bundle lands *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; `dump` and `mark` (DO-31); and channels for the 14 expected siblings, a **data-driven list** (`expected_channels`, kept with the composition root's dev registration code): Horror, Body, Lighting, Emitter, Layout, Save/Load, Interaction, Controller, Web Map, Audio, Assembly, Suit, In-World Instruments and HUD & Suit Layer (14; Boarding registers no channel, BS-1; Resolved 2026-10-27 — user ruling (design-review decisions): D98, OQ-DO-24), DO-12. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* *Vertical Slice B:* the seat cycler, the `MIRROR` own-seat view, `HOST_MIRROR_MISMATCH` (DO-34, AC-DO-44c) and the `harm_eligible` checks for class (c) refs (crewmates as horrors). *Later or spike:* the class (d) phantom-salvage channel, the second-window observer (spike S-3, AC-DO-78), dev `ConfigFile` persistence (OQ-DO-10) and the K-1 per-block hash column (P-10).

## Player Fantasy

*Indirect: the player never meets this system, and never will. They meet a promise it lets the studio keep.* (The register is the kernel's, kern:30. Two audiences are kept apart: the **player's** fantasy is written here, as trust; the **operator's** experience, the developer, QA or playtest observer who actually uses the tool, is written in Game Feel.)

**Every lie was meant, and every lie leaks.** The scrubber gauge in derelict A reads a calm OK while frost thickens on the elbow beside it. The player has to choose what to believe, and the game owes them one thing: the choice must be *fair*. The frost must be the ship, the gauge must be the dream, and the dream must show its seam somewhere (a gleam on the needle, a glide a real needle would not make). Pillar 1: the physical world is ground truth, only instruments and perception can be wrong. Pillar 4: no lie is unfalsifiable, and at least one channel of truth always remains.

A defect breaks that promise silently. A gauge that reads wrong because a value went stale, a rendering path skipped the Filter, or a co-op peer drifted out of step looks *exactly* like a hallucination to the player, but it has no cause, no leak and no way to be disproved. To the player that is not horror; it is the game cheating. The kernel says it plainly: "The fantasy has failed if any player-visible physical consequence ever disagrees with the authoritative state. That is a Pillar 1 violation, not a cosmetic bug — and the reason the Debug Overlay exists" (kern:42).

So the player's fantasy here is **trust in the doubt**: the confidence, earned across sorties, that suspicion is always worth acting on, because the world will answer. The Overlay is the instrument that earns it on the player's behalf. It puts the true value beside the perceived one and gives every difference a name (a lie, a returning lie, or a defect), so the studio can prove each lie was issued and leaked, and that each instrument's discrepancy matches its ledger entry, before any player has to take it on faith. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: narrowed. The Overlay checks instrument discrepancies. It has no probe for the physical consequence itself, the frost on the elbow or the hiss at the seam, so it cannot prove that each consequence agreed with the ship; that probe is logged as OQ-DO-27.)*

**Emotion target, across a player's run (what the Overlay protects, not what it adds):** *suspicion* (the snag in the reading) -> *a check that costs something* (walk to the elbow, feel the cold) -> *the world answers* -> *trust in the doubt* (next time the player acts on the snag sooner). This extends the Filter's arc (pf:59: "familiarity -> the snag -> suspicion with a direction -> quiet certainty -> trained eye"); the Overlay adds no emotion of its own to a run, it guarantees that the last two steps are never false.

**Corollary, the operator** *(the one sane seat aboard)*: the Overlay is the only place that sees both the ship and the dream. It is flat, unlit and plain, and it never lies, because it sits above the pass that does (pipe:102; pf:223 row 9). Its emotion arc is a report ("the gauge lied and I could not tell") -> reproduction -> classification in one glance (a named class, not a judgement call) -> certainty: a lie with a ledger entry and a leak is design, anything else is a ticket (written out in Game Feel).

**Rejected, by name, so nobody re-proposes it:** a "true sight" toggle, streamer mode, assist option or launch flag for curious players. An honest overlay over a lying HUD makes Pillar 4 switchable off (R-7, ab:886), and it fails six already-written release-export file-list acceptance criteria (kern:916, pipe:1028, AC-PF-79, player-controller-input.md:1071, AC-AT-66, AC-SN-85). Project Zomboid's `-debug` flag is an inspiration for the *principle* that debug is a separate mode with its own tools, never for the mechanism: a launch flag on a shipped binary ships the oracle.

**Anchor moment 1: the false-fixed scrubber (what the Overlay certifies)**

MVP slice, derelict A, sanity class (b) only (`CLASSES_ENABLED` {b}). A scrubber in compartment 2 has failed: its Graph node `hp` is 2400, below `HP_FAIL` 3000 (locked, Graph: `hp` 0..10000, `HP_OK` 7000 / `HP_FAIL` 3000). The player is at sanity stage 1 and Sanity issues a `FALSE_OK` lie on the scrubber's **state glyph**: the MVP scrubbers carry binary GLYPH and MACHINE_FRAME refs, and the `hp` needle (`strain`) is held out of the MVP manifest (in-world-instruments-and-inspect-view.md:227), so the glyph swaps to its running frame, with the gleam on it, while the frost and the falling temperature, which are honest (Pillar 1), say otherwise *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. In a dev build, `P-TRUTH` for that `STATE_GLYPH` ref with viewer PlayerId 1 shows the true value, the perceived `displayed_value`, `lie_id`, `lie_kind`, `leak_modes`, the ledger entry and the class. The same row distinguishes four cases, which is the whole mandate in one table (class names are DO-21's; the numeric worked example is in Formulas) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*:

| Variant | What differs | Class / severity | What it means for the player's fantasy |
|---|---|---|---|
| Lie issued, the leak (GLEAM) present | `ledger_active` (DO-21), `lie_id` non-zero, `leak_modes & GLEAM` set | `DELIBERATE` / `NOTE`, tagged `LIE` when the Filter's intent and the ref's scope are published (verified), and tagged `UNVERIFIED` when either is missing | A fair lie. The promise holds only when verified; an `UNVERIFIED` result is counted beside `defects seen` and is not the same promise. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| Not `ledger_active` (DO-21), `lie_id` 0, yet the glyph still shows the running frame | Nobody issued a lie; the value is simply wrong | `DEFECT_UNCAUSED` / `DEFECT` | The game cheated (a stale value, a desync between the Filter and its inputs). A ticket. A node that bypasses the Filter reads `DEFECT_BYPASS` and is visible only with the render probe present (DO-22). *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* |
| Lie issued, but the gleam never reaches the screen: it was dropped, fell under the Filter's amplitude floor, or was cut by an FX cap (`drawn_leak(ref)` 0 against a non-zero `leak_amplitude_pm`; a rising `leak_repaired` counter is a separate unattributed page event unless the Filter names the lie) | The lie has no seam on screen. (The Filter forces the GLEAM bit, PF-15, so the bit alone can never fail; the failure is in what is drawn.) | `DEFECT_NO_LEAK` / `DEFECT` | An unfalsifiable lie, a Pillar 4 violation. A ticket. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| Lie issued and the leak present, but the needle was never meant to leave the truth (the dial has no room, the intended departure is inside the tolerance, the needle is ineligible, or the true value is sweeping through an absolute target) | The Filter's own published intent says the departure is small or absent | `DELIBERATE` / `NOTE` tagged `LIE` plus `SHORT_REACH`, `SUB_TOL` or `NO_NEEDLE` | The Filter's designed fallback: gleam and sound carry the lie. Never a defect. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |

**Anchor moment 2: the playtest observer (the moment the Overlay is *used*)**

The MVP kill/continue playtest (X-BS-11, AC-BS-122). The tester boards derelict B and a creak runs through the hull. The build is the playtest preset, launched `HIDDEN` and staying `HIDDEN` for the whole session: nothing of the Overlay is drawn on the tester's machine. The observer, beside the tester, presses `mark` at the boarding and again when the creak runs through the hull (accepted silently in `HIDDEN`, no on-screen echo), and reads the **observer event log** (DO-35) on a second device or after the sortie: a timestamped line `ATTN_PHASE ... TELL` carries the tick, so the observer notes the tick and whether the tester reacted before the breach. At the end of the sortie the log, with the retained tester-screen capture, goes into `production/qa/evidence/`; the `FULL` pages and the strip are never opened on the tester's machine during a session. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the earlier reading, an observer toggling the strip beside the tester, put the stage and phase on the tester's screen and is withdrawn.)* This anchor is already a sibling acceptance criterion; it shows Pillars 2 and 3 being *measured* without a meter ever reaching a player; and it makes the oracle risk concrete: if the tester's screen, recording or stream ever shows `FULL` (or the strip, which names the stage and phase), the session's data is void.

*Rejected anchors:* the strip or `FULL` shown on the tester's machine at any point in a measured session; a second-window observer view (multi-window `Window` behaviour in 4.7 is `[UNV, HIGH]`; a spike only); an operator forcing a stage from the Overlay (`force_*` belongs to the owners' debug APIs, the Overlay is display only); any moment where a player sees the Overlay.

**The design test**

> **If a lie is only distinguishable from a defect with the Overlay open, the thing missing is a leak in the world, not a feature in the Overlay: fix the lie (PF-15), never extend the tool toward the player.**

*Corollary (the Pillar 1 test, "a hidden stat vs a visibly leaking pipe -> the pipe"):* if the Overlay is ever the only place a physical consequence can be read, the world is hiding a stat; fix the world.

*Fantasy failure condition (mirrors kern:42):* the fantasy has failed if a playtest report of "the gauge lied and I could not tell" cannot be classified on `P-TRUTH` as `DELIBERATE` or a named `DEFECT_*` within one reproduction, or if any publicly released build contains the Overlay (the supervised playtest preset, DO-6, is the one build that does, and it is `HIDDEN`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*.

**How the fantasy deepens, not breaks, under hallucination**

**The Overlay is immune by construction.** It sits at pipeline layer 4 above the Filter pass, and the frame is fed nothing (pipe:102: "no — it reads the true channel"; pf:223 row 9). It *does* read the perceived channel (only through the Filter's `perceived(ref, viewer)` pull; the Overlay does not read the `sanity.perception` block directly, which is read only by the Filter and the replication adapter), but only as data in the perceived column; it is never itself a filtered surface, and its colours never follow the lie (DO-27: true value Paper White, perceived Hull Grey, never violet or flesh even when the world lie leaks violet). There is no "the tool goes mad at stage 3" beat: a corruptible truth tool would destroy the one thing it exists for.

**What deepens is the player's Pillar 4 fantasy, which the Overlay certifies.** The harder the dream pushes, the more lies are in flight and the more the guarantee matters. The page does not get scarier; the guarantee gets stronger: at stage 3 with several lying refs the operator still answers "design or defect?" per row in one glance.

| Pillar 4 class | MVP status | What the Overlay certifies (owner surfaces only) | The defect it catches |
|---|---|---|---|
| (b) False system states | **Enabled** (`CLASSES_ENABLED` {b}) | Per ref: true vs perceived, ledger entry, leak modes, class (DO-21) | `DEFECT_UNCAUSED`, `DEFECT_NO_LEAK`, `DEFECT_SILENT_LIE`, `DEFECT_BYPASS` |
| (a) Phantom entities | Not enabled at MVP | Horror's own channel: true positions, state, locked point (horror-entities.md:378), registered under DO-12 and shown on `P-CHANNELS` | A hidden real entity with no footprint (Pillar 4: the hallucination hides the body, never the trail), via the owner's counters |
| (c) Crewmates as horrors | Vertical Slice B | `sanity_view(player_id)` `harm_eligible` per seat (contract only, DO-34) | Harm while the attacker was not eligible (an owner AC) |
| (d) Phantom salvage | Later | The owner's channel when designed | None designed here |

**Under the playtest, the tester's dread must stay intact (Pillar 2).** The observer modes exist to keep the truth *off* the tester's screen (`OBSERVER_STRIP`, `HIDDEN`, DO-6, DO-7). A tester who glimpses `FULL` has been handed the oracle Sanity warns about ("a deliberate Pillar 4 oracle risk", sn:174), and that session's data is void.

**Reference techniques: adopt and reject**

References are cited for technique, not as verified facts about those titles' current builds; none is load-bearing.

| Reference | Adopt | Reject |
|---|---|---|
| Minecraft's F3 debug screen | Text-first pages on one hotkey, fixed layout, no gameplay effect, readable at a glance | Release reachability: it ships to every player and became player meta (coordinates, biome hunting). Here that would be the oracle. |
| Factorio's debug options | Per-system world overlays toggled independently (outlines, cones, rects, true horror positions), each owned by the owning system's registered channel | Player-accessible toggles; per-system pages designed inside the tool (scope absorption) |
| Source-engine `net_graph` style readouts | The one-line compact strip for watching without reading | Showing it to the person being measured |
| Project Zomboid `-debug` | The principle that debug is a separate mode with its own tools | A launch flag on a shipped binary: it ships the oracle and anyone can type it |
| Celeste Assist Mode and accessibility toggles in general | Nothing for this system (Accessibility Settings owns the three Filter scalars, registry `filter_accessibility_scalars`) | The analogy itself: making a challenge switchable for a player is exactly what R-7 forbids when the challenge is Pillar 4 |
| Eternal Darkness / Amnesia style sanity meters | Nothing | Any sanity, attention or lie-count readout outside the Overlay root (PF-38, AC-AT-66, AC-SN-146) |
| Pandorum / Beyond the Aquila Rift (the "what was real" reveal) | Nothing for the Overlay; the reveal belongs to the world (the room gets cold, the crate was garbage all along) | Using the Overlay's true-beside-perceived layout as the template for any in-game "clarity" item, scanner mode or ending reveal (Pillar 1, Pillar 4, anti-pillar: no authored story) |

**Pillar and anti-pillar guard rails**

These are stated here as fantasy-level guard rails and made testable in Core Rules (DO-30, the Never list) and Acceptance Criteria.

| Guard rail | Source |
|---|---|
| **Never a player surface.** No setting, menu entry, "developer mode" toggle, rebinding entry, screen-reader node, tutorial line, achievement or launch flag in any publicly released build; the supervised playtest preset holds the Overlay `HIDDEN` for the whole session and registers no action that can leave it (DO-6, AC-DO-185) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling R16)*. | R-7; P-3, P-4, P-7 (Dependencies) |
| **Never a write surface.** No submit, mutator, `force_*` button, spawn, teleport, knob slider or rewind. Display only. | kern:117, kern:203; anti-pillar: not a freeform builder |
| **Never a meter outside its root.** No Attention level, sanity stage, lie count or verdict appears anywhere but the Overlay root. | Pillar 3 ("never as a hidden meter"), PF-38, AC-AT-66 |
| **Never corruptible.** Above the Filter pass; true value never tinted by the lie; no violet, no flesh; Alarm Red only as a mark beside a `DEFECT`; `NOT_USED` never red; `UNAVAILABLE` never shown as `HONEST`. | Pillar 1 (the sane palette is inviolable), pf:223 row 9, AC-PF-38 |
| **Never on the tester's screen in `FULL` or the strip.** Playtest builds are the playtest preset: launched `HIDDEN` by the preset's tag, not by a command-line option; the observer reads the non-screen observer log (DO-35). `OBSERVER_STRIP` is for the developer's own sessions. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | X-BS-11, AC-BS-122 |
| **Never audible.** No sonification, no beep on `DEFECT`; audio counters are displayed, never played. | Pillar 2 (audio tells belong to the world) |
| **Never part of the world.** Not a block, not in a snapshot, not in `state_hash`; Overlay on vs off yields an identical hash sequence. | AC-AT-56 shape |
| **Adds no verb and no knowledge.** Not a Pillar 5 "knowledge persists" channel, not a combat or PvP aid, not lore. What a player learns, they learn from the world. | Pillar 5; anti-pillars: not a combat game, no intentional PvP, no authored story |

## Detailed Design

### Core Rules

**Conventions.** Rules are `DO-n` (named); edge cases are `E-DO-nn`, acceptance criteria `AC-DO-nn`, tuning knobs `DIAG_*` / `OVERLAY_*`, all in the later sections. Tags follow the sibling GDDs: `[REF]` = in `docs/engine-reference/godot/`; `[UNV]` = unverified against the engine (the reference has **no** scripting, core, export or debugging file, so export presets and filters, feature tags, `OS.has_feature`, `OS.is_debug_build`, the `Performance` singleton, `Viewport.get_render_info`, `EngineDebugger` and multi-window `Window` are all `[UNV]`, knowledge-gap risk HIGH for 4.5 to 4.7); `[PLACEHOLDER]` = invented here, carrying a range and a proposed registry name, final nowhere. "-> ADR candidate" marks an implementation choice this GDD does not make. Every name from another GDD is used verbatim; a sibling's *provisional* name is cited with its source line and the word "provisional" and is never restated as a fact. Each rule gives the *Rule*, the *Why* and the acceptance-criteria group that proves it. Where a specialist proposal and a ruling differed, a one-line "(orchestrator ruling: ...)" note says which was taken.

**Reading order.** Authority and gate: DO-1 (display only), DO-2 (build gate and source root), DO-3 (one subscriber), DO-9 (no sim state). Structure and layer: DO-4 (model/view), DO-5 (layer 4). Modes, input and viewer: DO-6 to DO-8, DO-10. Kernel states and boundaries: DO-11, DO-32 to DO-34. The channel and page contract: DO-12 to DO-15, DO-26. Reads, hashes, timings and counters: DO-16 to DO-19. The true/perceived pair and the discriminator: DO-20 to DO-23. The Sanity and Attention pages: DO-24, DO-25. Presentation, inertness, the Never list and evidence: DO-27 to DO-31. The non-screen observer sink and the defect ring: DO-35 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. The channel contract, classifier and seven page tables follow the rules.

**DO-1. Display-only `DIAGNOSTIC` reader.** *Rule:* The Overlay is a kernel subscriber of class `DIAGNOSTIC`. It reads views and the kernel's diagnostics surface and writes nothing. It submits no command, calls no mutator, calls no owner's debug write function, and owns no block, no hook, no read view and no RNG stream. `force_stage`, `force_level`, Suit forcing and runtime-knob changes (honoured at P6, kern:734) belong to the owners' debug APIs, reached from the dev console (owed by the lead-programmer; kern:734, player-controller-input.md:742, suit-and-equipment.md:1317) behind the DO-2 gate; the Overlay only *displays* their result (a `FORCED` tag, DO-24). *Why:* kern:117 ("Subscriber handlers run with writes disabled... `INPUT_SURFACE` is the one class that may submit"); kern:203 row 11 ("Neither gets submit or mutators"); CR-9. A button that forced a stage would make the Overlay a write surface and break CR-9's `WriteScope` rule. *(orchestrator ruling: display only. Routed: Sanity's own row, sn:1352, lists `force_stage` / `force_level` beside the Overlay; a one-clause clarification is requested, "reached from the dev console, displayed by the Overlay". The tension between a debug-only `force_*` and CR-9 is the kernel owner's question and exists regardless of this GDD.)* *AC group:* inertness, gating.

**DO-2. One gate, one dev source root.** *Rule:* All Overlay code, scenes, the world debug-draw node and the Overlay's dev actions live under **two source roots** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* `[PLACEHOLDER]`, final paths owned by architecture: the **observer root** `src/observer/` (the Overlay model, view, the observer sink and the Overlay's own dev actions: the only dev code the playtest preset includes; proposed registry name `observer_source_root`) and the **dev root** `src/debug/` (every other dev seam: `force_*`, the dev console, the dev input logger, the tree-walk script, the flash-scan tool and the owner-side channel providers; proposed registry name `overlay_source_root`). The public release excludes both roots and the playtest preset excludes only `src/debug/`, by export resource filters `[UNV, HIGH]`. The Overlay has its own root so that the playtest preset never needs a nested include of a folder inside an excluded one: Godot's precedence for that case is `[UNV]`, and if it were wrong the failure would be silent and unsafe (testers would receive `force_*` and the console). **Owner-side providers** (the code a system writes to implement `pull()` and its `draws[]` callbacks) live under `src/debug/<system>/`, never in the owner's runtime module, and the Input Adapter registers the dev actions through a dev-only seam `register_dev_forwarder` (requested of the Controller, OQ-DO-6), so no runtime module names a dev path outside that seam. The composition root registers the `DIAGNOSTIC` subscription entry point and the dev-only actions **only** when the dev predicate holds: the built-in `editor` feature or the custom feature tag `dev` (placeholders, `[UNV]`; a custom tag reads false in an editor run, so the predicate includes `editor`, verified in S-2). A second custom tag, `playtest`, selects the playtest preset (DO-6), which registers the `DIAGNOSTIC` subscription, the observer sink and the single action `debug_overlay_mark`, and nothing else (`debug_overlay_reveal` is dev-only) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling R16)*. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* A CI step lists the public release export's files and fails the build on any path under either root; a second step compares the playtest export's file list to the playtest allow-list as an exact set (AC-DO-170). "Zero cost in Release" therefore means *nothing loaded*, not a runtime branch. **Load discipline:** the composition root loads the Overlay root only through a runtime `load()` guarded by `ResourceLoader.exists()` on the dev-root path; no `preload`, no `class_name` and no scene or resource reference to a dev-root path exists outside the dev root, and the dev root itself declares no `class_name` (a `preload` or a `class_name` in the filtered root breaks the release export, including through a stale global class cache). Lint AC-DO-172; a release export booted with the root absent is AC-DO-171. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* The gate, the root and the CI step are **shared** by every dev seam guarded elsewhere (`force_*`, `--filter-test-freeze-dither`, `IA_PHANTOM_PROBE`, the dev input-event logger, the tree-walk script and the flash-scan tool, the dev console, the Input Adapter's `PC_*` presentation-diagnostics surface and its `debug_overlay_*` action registration); proposed registry name `overlay_build_gate`, and one versioned list of known dev artefacts (proposed `dev_artifact_list`) that every file-list check reuses. *Why:* six already-written acceptance criteria inspect the release export **file list**, not a runtime branch: kern:916, pipe:1028, AC-PF-79 (pf:1846), player-controller-input.md:1071, AC-AT-66 (at:1572) and AC-SN-85. A runtime `if OS.is_debug_build()` fails all six, and a flag can be flipped and still ship the oracle (Pillar 4, R-7). The same root makes the lints that allow-list "the Debug Overlay" implementable (AC-AT-66, AC-PF-51, AC-SN-74, AC-SN-146). *(orchestrator ruling: file-level exclusion plus gated registration; a runtime flag alone and a separate-project conditional compile were rejected, GDScript having no `#if`.)* *Engine gap:* export filters and feature tags are `[UNV, HIGH]`; one spike story (release-export an empty scene plus one file under the root, list the files, record `OS.has_feature` results in editor, dev export, playtest export and release export, and the tag semantics of each). -> ADR candidate (debug-gate / export-filter). *AC group:* absence and gating.

**DO-3. Exactly one subscriber.** *Rule:* There is one `DIAGNOSTIC` subscription for the whole Overlay, with the DO-12 channel registry behind it, and therefore one entry point to strip. The handler only **copies** the P5 notification batch `(block_id, tick, change_keys)` (CR-10) and marks the model dirty (one batch per block per tick in a normal frame; in a catch-up frame, one merged batch per block, stamped with the frame's last tick and carrying the union of its change keys, because the Overlay is a `DIAGNOSTIC` subscriber and the kernel's ratified R-12 merges `PRESENTATION`, `PROJECTOR` and `DIAGNOSTIC` batches per catch-up frame, 2026-10-27; the batch feeds only the dirty-key accounting of DO-18, every other figure is read from the kernel `diagnostics` accessor or the owners' views, so the Overlay depends on no intermediate tick's batch) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*; it does no formatting, no classification and no I/O. TellAudit (AT-15) stays Attention's own `DIAGNOSTIC` fixture; the Overlay only displays its result (DO-25). *Why:* one entry point to exclude from release (DO-2); handlers must be cheap (kern:114); a handler that aborts on an engine script error is isolated by the kernel's best-effort completion sentinel (CR-19(b)): the handler returns `ctx.complete()`, a call that returns without it is recorded `INCOMPLETE` against the subscriber id and the kernel stays `RUNNING` with `T` advanced; engine script errors cannot be intercepted (CR-19(c)), so isolation is best-effort plus lint. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: replaces the kern:867 'throwing subscriber' wording, which CR-19 superseded)* *(orchestrator ruling: one subscriber, not one per panel or per system.)* *AC group:* gating, panel-fault isolation.

**DO-4. Model/view split, pure model.** *Rule:* A pure `OverlayModel` (no scene tree) is built from `(diagnostics snapshot, read views, registered channels)`; an `OverlayView` Control tree pulls from the model at render rate. The classifier and every formula are model functions, unit-testable headless under `tests/unit/debug_overlay/`. The model holds **copies**, never a live reference to a block (GDScript has no const references, so a read-only view over a block is a copy or a frozen wrapper; kernel OQ 8). The model performs **no `FileAccess` I/O**: observer-log lines go to an injected `ObserverSink` (seam T-7) that owns every file operation, so the model stays headless-testable and DO-4's purity holds (AC-DO-186) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. This GDD describes behaviour (what is pure, what is cached, what is copied), not class code. -> ADR candidate (node and class layout, copy strategy). *Why:* testability (coding standards: dependency injection over singletons); writes-disabled is a kernel-enforced property, not the Overlay's; the Overlay must not hold a reference that could be mutated through. *AC group:* classifier, pages.

**DO-5. Layer 4, the truth channel.** *Rule:* The view is a root-viewport `CanvasLayer` at pipeline layer 4, integer-scaled at the HUD scale (`H_default = max(k, 3)`, pipe:224 (PR-12), pipe:373) plus the placeholder `OVERLAY_SCALE_OFFSET`, above the HUD (layer 2) and chart table and menus (layer 3). It receives **no** Filter pass, no lighting, no camera jolt and no grade (pipe:102; pf:223 row 9: the pass sits below every `CanvasLayer`, the frame is fed nothing). It carries no Filter-fed value except the `perceived` column, which is labelled as perceived. Non-interactive panels use `MOUSE_FILTER_IGNORE`; the Overlay never takes gameplay focus (4.6 dual focus `[REF modules/ui.md]`; the 4.5 recursive mouse-filter behaviour is also in the reference). **Process model** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: the Overlay root and its model driver run with `process_mode = PROCESS_MODE_ALWAYS` and never depend on `SceneTree.paused`; the numeral refresh, the class fold and the view update run from the Overlay's own `_process`, so a paused game (the kernel `PAUSED`, or a tree paused by a menu) stays inspectable. `Engine.time_scale` scales the process delta that accumulates toward the next numeral refresh, so numerals refresh more slowly in slow motion, but **neither `Engine.time_scale` nor the refresh throttle ever changes a classification**, because the class fold and every counter run on kernel ticks (DO-21). The Overlay's `process_priority` is set after the kernel's TickDriver node (CR-1a), so a refresh reads committed state. *Why:* the truth channel must not itself be corruptible (Pillar 1); the Overlay is one of the surfaces the Filter pass never reaches (pf:223 row 9). *AC group:* palette, gating, focus.

**DO-6. Four modes and two presets.** (registry: `overlay_modes`) *Rule:* `OFF` (registered, handler returns at once, nothing collected), `FULL` (all pages), `OBSERVER_STRIP` (one line, DO-7) and `HIDDEN` (subscribed and collecting the observer set only, drawing nothing; DO-35); transitions in States and Transitions. A mode change takes effect within one rendered frame and never alters `T`. **Two export presets, one source tree** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: the **dev preset** (the full dev root under the dev predicate of DO-2) launches `OFF` by default, or `HIDDEN` with the launch option placeholder `--overlay=hidden`, and registers every dev action. The **playtest preset** is the release template plus the custom feature tag `playtest`: the composition root forces `HIDDEN` at launch whatever the command line says (a store or double-click launch cannot pass an option, so tester safety no longer depends on one), registers **only** `debug_overlay_mark` (no `reveal`, no `toggle`, no `mode`, no `dump`), so a tester session can never leave `HIDDEN` (Resolved 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling R16, OQ-DO-30 option A: `debug_overlay_reveal` is dev-only and absent from the playtest export's action map), and starts the observer sink (DO-35). The playtest export contains the Overlay root and the sink but none of the other dev seams (`force_*`, the dev console, the dev input logger); an allow-list check of its own file list is AC-DO-170. The user confirmed the release template as the base of the playtest preset on 2026-10-27 (D97); that the kernel `diagnostics` accessor exists in it is still verified in spike S-2 and recorded in the debug-gate ADR *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* (fallback if the accessor is absent from the release template: the debug template with the same tag). *Why:* X-BS-11, AC-BS-122 and AC-SN-88 need an observer read that is never shown to the tester. *(orchestrator ruling, revised at design review: the earlier "a playtest build is a dev build launched `HIDDEN`; there is no third build flavour in MVP" is replaced by a preset; it is still one source tree and one code path. Resolved 2026-10-27 — user ruling (design-review decisions): the playtest export preset is confirmed (D97, OQ-DO-25), and the earlier "no third build flavour in MVP" ruling is superseded by the user.)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* *AC group:* modes and observer.

**DO-7. Observer strip and the oracle rule.** *Rule:* `OBSERVER_STRIP` is one text line: `T`, kernel state, Attention `phase`, the viewer's sanity `stage` and hash status (`OK` / `n/a` / `DRIFT` plus the age of `hash_T`). It also shows the sticky `defects seen` count of DO-35 (a number, no ref), the `unverified` count and the coverage digits (`intent n/N, leak-probe n/N, bypass-probe n/N`, DO-21 (5)(v); numbers only, no ref) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. It shows no ledger field, no true/perceived pair and no ref; the hash status is the age of `hash_T` with the algorithm tag as DO-17 defines it (the kernel's `hash_algo` field, granted 2026-10-27; `H64 (algo untagged; SHA-256/8)` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings, consistency follow-up)* only if it is absent). `FULL` and the strip are **tester-hostile**: a tester's screen, recording or stream must never carry either (the strip names the stage and the phase). `HIDDEN` is only safe because leaving it needs the reveal chord (DO-8), which exists in the dev preset only; in the playtest preset no action leaves `HIDDEN` at all *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling R16)*. In a measured playtest session the strip is not used at all: the observer reads the log (DO-35). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *Why:* sn:174 calls the ledger "a deliberate Pillar 4 oracle risk"; the Overlay is the only reader outside `sanity/` (AC-SN-74). The risk is a process risk the code can only mitigate, not remove, and the GDD says so plainly. *AC group:* modes and observer (the strip contains no ref and no ledger field; `HIDDEN` draws no Overlay node).

**DO-8. Toggle input path and reveal chord.** *Rule:* The Controller's Input Adapter registers the Overlay's dev-only actions only under the DO-2 dev predicate (in the playtest preset only `debug_overlay_mark`, DO-6; `debug_overlay_reveal` and its chords are dev-only, delegated ruling R16) and forwards them to the Overlay by a host-local call: never a command, never a binding-table entry visible to Settings, never in the rebinding or accessibility enumerations. Placeholder names (registry: `debug_overlay_actions`): `debug_overlay_toggle`, `debug_overlay_page_next`, `debug_overlay_page_prev`, `debug_overlay_mode`, `debug_overlay_dump`, and two added here, `debug_overlay_reveal` and `debug_overlay_mark`, and three for selection: `debug_overlay_cursor_next`, `debug_overlay_cursor_prev` (move a row cursor over the current page's selectable rows: refs and the viewer row on `P-TRUTH`, channels and draw toggles on `P-CHANNELS`) and `debug_overlay_activate` (pin or unpin the ref under the cursor, cycle the viewer, select the channel, flip the draw toggle); all three act only in `FULL`. Placeholder defaults *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: F5 to F8 moved off the editor's run, pause and stop shortcuts)*: toggle F3, page next and previous F4 and Shift+F4, mode cycle Insert, dump Home, mark End, cursor next and previous PageDown and PageUp, activate Delete, reveal chord Ctrl+Shift+F3; gamepad: the reveal chord is both stick clicks (L3 + R3, as the Controller records it), recognised by a **stateful detector in the Adapter** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: both clicks must be down within a 0.5 s chord window of each other, held 0.5 s, fire once, and re-arm only after both are released (the Adapter counts these in its own injected frame deltas; the Overlay reads no clock); other gamepad bindings `[PLACEHOLDER]`; every default is verified against the editor's shortcuts and the Controller's gameplay table `[UNV]` (OQ-DO-6). The row cursor is dev-session state (DO-9), wraps at the ends of the page's selectable rows, and `activate` on a row with no action is ignored. In `HIDDEN` only the reveal chord (dev preset only; the playtest preset registers no `reveal`, R16) and `mark` act: the chord reveals to `OBSERVER_STRIP`, not `FULL`; `mark` is accepted silently and echoed to the log (DO-35). From `OBSERVER_STRIP` the same chord leads to `FULL` with the history preserved, the only route from the strip to `FULL`, so a stray `toggle` never clears history. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **Matching** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: every binding uses exact-match semantics (`InputEvent.is_match(event, true)`), so Shift+F4 never also fires F4 and Ctrl+Shift+F3 never also fires `toggle` (which could clear the history the chord was meant to reveal); the Adapter resolves one event to one action, longest modifier set first, and ignores echo events (`event.is_echo()`), so a held key never repeats an action. The actions are `StringName` action names and use `InputEvent.DEVICE_ID_*`, not `device == 0` `[REF breaking-changes.md 4.7]`. *Why:* the Controller lint "no `Input` reads outside the Adapter" (player-controller-input.md:58 PC-1, :1026); a tester brushing the plain toggle must not reveal the oracle. *(orchestrator ruling: the Adapter-forwarded dev action set, plus the reveal chord, the mark action and the three selection actions as refinements; the Controller-owner amendment request grows by five action names and joins the open lint collision at player-controller-input.md:1097.)* *AC group:* input path, enumeration negatives.

**DO-9. Dev-session state only.** *Rule:* Mode, page, row cursor, selected viewer, selected ref, pinned refs, observer marks, the defect ring (DO-35) and every history window are dev-session state: never a block, never in a snapshot, never in `state_hash`, never saved across launches in MVP (a dev `ConfigFile` under `user://` is deferred, Open Questions). *Why:* a persisted Overlay block would enter `state_hash` and break the G4 comparison with the Overlay on versus off (AC-AT-56 shape). *AC group:* inertness.

**DO-10. One viewer.** *Rule:* The Overlay has one selectable viewer `PlayerId`, defaulting to the local seat (MVP: PlayerId 1) and always shown in the header; the operator cycles it with `activate` on the viewer row of `P-TRUTH`, reached with `cursor_next` / `cursor_prev` (DO-8), and each cycle resets the per-`(ref, viewer)` counters (AC-DO-77). Addressing is by `PlayerId`, never `peer_id` (pf:271, PF-10). Because the Filter answers for the **local seat only** and returns the honest record for a remote `PlayerId` while counting `remote_queries` (PF-10), the Overlay **never calls `perceived` for a remote seat**: a viewer other than the local seat classifies `UNAVAILABLE` with reason `REMOTE_VIEWER`, and `P-TRUTH` shows "perceived: not queried". This keeps `remote_queries` a pure leakage indicator and prevents a seat-B lie from classifying `HONEST` against its own honest-record answer. Vertical Slice B adds a seat cycler (contract only, DO-34). *(orchestrator ruling: one selectable viewer, refined by the finding that the Filter answers for the local seat only, PF-10.)* *AC group:* classifier, viewer switch.

**DO-11. Behaviour in every kernel state.** *Rule:* The Overlay is drawable in all six kernel states (table in States and Transitions). In a dev build `FAULTED` halts the kernel with diagnostics (kern:151); the render loop and the Overlay keep running and show the FAULT record `(T, type, issuer, host_order, block_id, assertion)` while the kernel is not ticking (chart-table-web-map-and-menus.md:377 accepts "Dev builds halt with the Debug Overlay (not ours)" as written). `BOOTING` shows boot progress and, on failure, the unknown `hook_id` or the cycle path (kern:606). `HIDDEN` stays hidden even on `FAULTED`: a tester must not see it either. *Why:* the Overlay is the tool one reaches for when the kernel is least healthy. *AC group:* kernel states.

**DO-12. Channel registration contract.** *Rule:* Every system that wants a diagnostics presence registers a **channel** at composition-root time under the DO-2 tag. A channel declares the fields in the contract table below (proposed registry name `diag_panel_contract`). **Entry point** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: the single registration call is `register_channel(declaration: Dictionary) -> Dictionary` on the Overlay's composition-root facade, valid only while the kernel is `BOOTING` and returning `{ok: bool, reason: String}`, where `reason` is a `DIAG_ERR` value (DO-14) or `LATE_REGISTRATION`, the one registration-time reason outside `DIAG_ERR` (a refused call, not a faulting channel: it raises no `DEFECT` row); a call after `BOOTING` returns `{ok: false, reason: "LATE_REGISTRATION"}` and the channel is `REJECTED`. The Overlay renders whatever is registered on the generic page `P-CHANNELS` and restates no owner's name as a fact. *Why:* it closes the three open asks "the Debug Overlay GDD should freeze the names" (pipe:1055, player-controller-input.md:1095, interaction-system.md:731) as **owners freeze their own names, using this convention**, and honours Body's "use the exact diagnostic names above" (ship-body-and-interior-physics.md:898) by construction. Freezing six systems' tables here would repeat the 2026-10-26 placeholder-becomes-fact failure and go stale on the first sibling edit. Transport and format of any diagnostics pipeline stay the AT-32 ADR candidate. *(orchestrator ruling: panel contract and naming convention here; names stay with their owners.)* *AC group:* channel contract.

**DO-13. Naming convention and severity vocabulary.** *Rule:* Channel and row names are snake_case `<system>_<noun>` or the owner's existing prefix (`PC_*`, `IA_*`, `BDY_*`, `graph_*`, `diag.*`, all provisional where their owner says so). Units are in the declaration, never in the name; a counter name does not end in `_ms` or `_us` unless the owner's registry name already does. The severity vocabulary is fixed at four levels: `OK`, `NOTE`, `WARN`, `DEFECT` (registry: `diag_severity_vocabulary`). A row declares its severity as a rule over its own value (for example "`> 0` is `WARN`"); the Overlay evaluates the rule and never invents one for an owner. A `DEFECT` rule must cite the owner's rule it enforces. *Why:* this resolves the mismatch between the brief's `OK/WARN/DEFECT` and `OK/NOTE/DEFECT`. Severity is always a text tag and colour is secondary (DO-27). *AC group:* channel contract.

**DO-14. Per-panel fault isolation as validated results.** *Rule:* GDScript has no exceptions: a script error aborts the function it occurs in and, under `-d`, stops the debugger (kernel CR-19(c)), so a channel fault is defined as an **invalid result**, never as a catch. Every channel `pull()` returns a plain Dictionary `{channel_id, rows, complete}`, and every page render consumes only validated data. The Overlay validates each result and each registration against a **closed error enum `DIAG_ERR`**: `NULL_RESULT` (null; also what an aborted `pull()` returns), `NOT_A_DICTIONARY`, `SCHEMA_MISMATCH` (a missing or mistyped field, an unknown row kind or an unknown severity word), `LIMIT_EXCEEDED` (`DIAG_ROWS_PER_CHANNEL_MAX` or a text or table cap), `INVALID_CALLABLE` (`Callable.is_valid()` false at registration or at call time) and `INCOMPLETE` (`complete` is not `true`: the kernel's completion-sentinel convention, CR-19(b)). The failing channel shows one `DEFECT` row naming its `channel_id` and the `DIAG_ERR` value, and after `DIAG_PANEL_ERR_LATCH_N` consecutive failures the channel is suspended until the next mode change. Real script errors (a nested helper that errors, a null dereference) are printed by the engine and belong to the debugger and to CI, which fails on any engine `ERROR` line in a dev run; the Overlay claims no more than the kernel does (best-effort plus lint). An expected channel not yet registered during `BOOTING` shows `PENDING`, and after `BOOTING` shows `NOTE absent`; a channel with no update for `DIAG_CHANNEL_STALE_S` shows `STALE` (`NOTE`). A diagnostics tool must not take the build down. *Why:* the kernel's fault channel (CR-19) is the precedent and replaced the earlier 'throws' model for the same reason; kern:867's old 'throwing subscriber' wording is superseded by CR-19, so no cross-document escalation is outstanding. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *AC group:* panel fault.

**DO-15. Page set.** *Rule:* Seven MVP pages, each specified as a data table of (row, source, unit, cadence, severity rule), below: `P-KERNEL`, `P-HOOKS`, `P-STATE`, `P-TRUTH`, `P-SANITY`, `P-ATTENTION`, `P-FRAME`. Every other system appears only as a registered channel on the generic page `P-CHANNELS` (a channel list plus the selected channel's rows and its world debug-draw toggles); that is not an eighth designed panel. Page order is fixed and `page_next` / `page_prev` cycle `P-KERNEL` to `P-FRAME` and then `P-CHANNELS`. No page designs a layout; layout is the view's affair. *Why:* the seven pages cover the six routed items (hash, dirty keys, hook timings, the Graph `true_value`, Attention level, sanity per player) plus the PF-41 contract and the Pipeline surface while keeping a system sized S. *AC group:* pages.

**DO-16. Reads only published names.** *Rule:* The Overlay reads a system only through that system's published read view or diagnostics channel: Graph `true_value(kind, id)`, `block_hash()`, `summary()`, `failures(filter)`, `compartment(cid)`, `power_use(section_id)` (lsg:199); Suit `true_value(key, player_id)` (SE-29); Sanity `sanity_view(player_id)`, `sanity.state`, `sanity.ledger` (SN-28, **sn:335-337**, cited as the paragraph, not the bare sn:336); Attention `attention.state`, `attention.view`; Horror `horror.view`; the Filter's `perceived` and the PF-41 counters. It never reaches into a block it does not own a view of. The ledger is `HOST_ONLY` (provisional on a mirror, DO-34): on a `MIRROR` the ledger is unreadable and rows needing it classify `UNAVAILABLE` with reason `NO_LEDGER`. *Why:* the ledger lint (AC-SN-74) names the Overlay as the one non-Sanity reader; the Overlay is the sole sanctioned `true_value` caller beside the Filter (AC-PF-51). *AC group:* gating (lint), cross-system.

**DO-17. Hash display: locked versus provisional.** *Rule:* The Overlay shows the kernel's `state_hash` exactly as last published, with `hash_T` (the tick it was computed at), its age `T - hash_T` and the algorithm tag. **`hash_algo` is not a kernel-published name** (the kernel names only `H64`, kern:531, D.12; the kernel text "xxHash64 or FNV-1a 64, pending ADR" is **superseded**: H64 is ratified as the first 8 bytes of SHA-256 (user ruling X13, R-17) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings, consistency follow-up)*; a search of the kernel GDD finds no `hash_algo`): it was a **field this GDD requested** in the P-9 accessor (DO-33a), granted by the kernel 2026-10-27; if it is absent the algorithm cell reads `H64 (algo untagged; SHA-256/8)` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings, consistency follow-up)*. With the field present, a hash from another `hash_algo` is never compared. The per-block column is the owner-published `block_hash()` where one exists (Graph LS-19 and LS-21, Emitter `block_hash()`, Layout `layout_hash`) and `n/a` otherwise; if the kernel adopts K-1 (a per-block cached hash, **routed, not adopted**, lsg:1155; sl:1290) the column switches to the kernel's per-block hash (P-10). The Overlay **never hashes anything itself**, on a timer or on demand. The cadence is the kernel's (DO-33). *Why:* K-1 is "routed, not adopted; D.12 stands until the kernel owner amends" (lsg:1155), and OQ-17 (sl:1316) warns that a GDScript 64-bit hash over 100 to 300 KiB may exceed 1.5 ms, so a hash per tick would put 90 ms of hashing into every real second. *Split:* the `state_hash` formula (kern D.12) is locked; per-block hashing and the cadence are provisional. *(orchestrator ruling: the kernel computes, the Overlay displays.)* *AC group:* pages (hash), unblocked sibling ACs (kern:915).

**DO-18. Dirty-key accounting with peak-hold.** *Rule:* In the P5 batch the handler records, per delivered batch `(block_id, tick)`, the key count (or an `ALL` collapse, kern D.9). In a normal frame that is one sample per tick; in a catch-up frame (kernel R-12, ratified by the user 2026-10-27) it is one merged sample stamped with the frame's last tick whose keys are the union over the frame's ticks, so it can read higher than any single tick did and can collapse to `ALL` where no single tick would; such a collapse is `WARN`, never `DEFECT` (the merge is legal kernel behaviour, not a fault), and the Overlay does not try to reconstruct per-tick counts. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* Each refresh the model folds the batches delivered since the last refresh into `last`, `peak` and `collapse_count` per block. `dirty_load_pm` (Formulas) is shown against `MAX_DIRTY_KEYS` 256, **never altered**. An `ALL` collapse is `WARN`, not `DEFECT`: it is legal kernel behaviour, and a restore legitimately produces one `ALL` then silence. The Graph's measured worst cases (about 38 keys MVP, about 135 VS-A, lsg:201 LS-20) are the reference points. Every `DEFECT`-capable counter and timing is **peak-held** across the folded ticks, never sampled: numerals refresh at `DIAG_REFRESH_HZ` (10) but the kernel emits at `TICK_RATE` 60 and the Graph hooks run on ticks 6n, 6n + 2 and 6n + 4 *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*, so a one-tick spike would otherwise fall between refreshes. *Why:* a cadence-blind display would hide the very events it exists to catch. *AC group:* pages (dirty keys, peak-hold).

**DO-19. Hook timings: registry names, indicative on the dev template.** *Rule:* `P-HOOKS` prints the kernel's resolved hook order (kern:607: "printed by the Debug Overlay"; identical every boot) and each hook's `t_hook` as the kernel publishes it, plus the kernel's `step_over_budget` flag, **displayed, not recomputed** (kern D.6; `STEP_BUDGET_MS` 4 is "a threshold, not a cap"). It adds one derived quantity: `graph_hook_tick(T) = sum of t_hook(h, T) over the Graph hooks h with hook_runs_this_tick(h, T)` (one hook per tick at the baseline stagger: 6n runs `graph.propagate.power`, 6n + 2 `graph.propagate.atmo`, 6n + 4 `graph.propagate.failure`), with a rolling P99 and a maximum over `DIAG_HOOK_WINDOW_RUNS` hook-tick samples (`hook_window_p99`, Formulas) compared with `GRAPH_HOOK_BUDGET_MS` (registry `GRAPH_HOOK_BUDGET_MS`: 0.5 P99 / 1.0 max per hook tick (MVP); 1.0 P99 per tick (VS-A)) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*. **The figure is indicative:** the budget is defined on the **Release export template** where the Overlay is absent, so the Overlay's number is a dev-template number; the gate is the Graph's S1 spike and perf gate. A breach of the indicative figure is `WARN`, never `DEFECT`. **One sample per hook tick; template-aware verdict** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*: a sample is every tick on which at least one Graph hook ran and reported a `t_hook`; it is keyed `(T, restore epoch)`, so a tick with no Graph hook, a pause or a restore straddle, or a duplicate delivery of the same `(T, epoch)` is dropped and counted, never sampled, and a duplicate cannot enter twice. At the baseline stagger the three hooks never share a tick, so each hook tick is its own sample and a 310 us "run" sum never exists. The verdict is compared with `GRAPH_HOOK_BUDGET_MS` only on the Release template (the playtest preset); on the dev (debug) template the figure is shown labelled "dev template: indicative, no verdict" unless a calibration scale `DIAG_DEV_TEMPLATE_SCALE_PM` is supplied by the Graph's S1 spike (default 0, no verdict), so a debug build is not given a permanent `WARN`. The maximum is judged by the number of samples over the max budget, not by one outlier (F-3). The Overlay reads no wall clock of its own for these (the kernel's diagnostics timer is the one allow-listed reader, H9, kern:781). *Why:* the budget is a P99 of the *sum* of the Graph hooks that run on one tick, while the kernel exposes per-hook `t_hook` only; the reflexion log (2026-10-26, two-bound formula) says to register the formula, not only its constants. The registry name wins over the Graph's row label for the same quantity (lsg:724): the Overlay never surfaces a second, differently named constant. *AC group:* pages (hooks), performance.

**DO-20. Ref picker and the true/perceived pair.** *Rule:* **The classified set is separate from the displayed list** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. The **classified set** is every ref the class fold classifies: the loaded instrument refs (Instruments' `instrument_diag(ref)` rows, one per loaded instrument, E-IW-49, bounded by `INSTRUMENT_SHARE_MAX` 24; the enumeration is OQ-DO-26 (C)), plus the refs the Filter has been asked about this session, plus pinned refs, under an explicit cap `DIAG_FOLD_REFS_MAX`. A node that bypasses the Filter and never calls `perceived` is still in the set, because the Overlay calls `perceived(ref, viewer)` for every ref in it (a hit on the Filter's memo or a first evaluation); such a node is in the set so that the probe can check it. The classifier cannot see the bypass itself, because `perceived(ref, viewer)` and `true_value` stay honest for it: with the render probe present (DO-22) the ref reaches `DEFECT_BYPASS` (row 5); with the probe absent its class is the one its perceived, true and ledger inputs give, `bypass_check` reads `ABSENT`, no defect is raised, and the only protection is the coverage readout `bypass-probe 0/N` (DO-21 (5)(v)), which reads as "bypass not checkable", never as clean *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*. Overflow past the cap raises a visible `N unclassified` NOTE in the header and in the `COVERAGE` log line and is never silent. `DIAG_TRUTH_REFS_MAX` limits **only the `P-TRUTH` rows**: the displayed list is the first `DIAG_TRUTH_REFS_MAX` refs of the classified set, ordered first every ref for which `ledger_active` holds (DO-21), then the refs the Filter has been asked about this session, then pinned refs; a `DEFECT` on a ref that is not displayed still counts in the header severity, the ring and the log. The operator moves the row cursor over this list with `cursor_next` / `cursor_prev`; the ref under the cursor is the selected ref, and `activate` pins or unpins it (DO-8); a pin is dev-session state (DO-9). For the selected ref, in **one rendered frame**, the model reads (this read belongs to the class fold, which performs it itself at the Sanity-run cadence whatever the numeral refresh or its throttle does; the numeral refresh displays the last fold), in this order and before any other callback: `tv` from the owner (Graph `true_value(kind, id)` for `GRAPH_NODE` and `GRAPH_COMPARTMENT`; Suit `true_value(key, player_id)` for `SUIT_KEY`; the Graph node state for `STATE_GLYPH`, `HOUSING_GLOW` and `MACHINE_FRAME` through the Filter's ref-to-subject mapping, *provisional* per PF-9; `SCANNER` has no published true source and is `UNAVAILABLE` with reason `NO_TRUE_SOURCE` until the Filter publishes one), then `p = perceived(ref, viewer)` (a hit on the Filter's per-`(ref, viewer, frame)` memo, pf:247, allocation-free on the honest path), then the matching ledger entry and the Filter counters. It shows side by side: the true value, the perceived `displayed_value` and `displayed_pm`, `lie_id`, `lie_kind`, `leak_modes` (GLEAM / GLIDE / SOUND), `leak_amplitude_pm`, `motion_mode`, the ledger `start_run` and `end_run`, `disc`, `tol`, the class and its severity. The true value is never tinted by the lie. **Ordering** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: rescoped)*: Godot runs physics steps before `_process`, and the kernel's tick driver is one `PROCESS_MODE_ALWAYS` node stepping from its own `_process` (CR-1a, which closes the tick-source question; the physics step is not the clock), so the real exposure is within a frame: the Overlay reads `T` once at the start of a refresh, sets its `process_priority` after the driver node, and **checks explicitly** (an `if`, never `assert()`, which release templates strip and the playtest preset is one) that every read of that refresh carries that `T`, incrementing a `read_straddle` counter shown on `P-KERNEL` (`DEFECT` above 0) when one does not *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; a catch-up frame that stepped `N` ticks (`N` up to `MAX_TICKS_PER_FRAME` 4) is read after all `N`. It asks the Filter to publish its memo key `(ref_key, viewer, frame_id)` so a memo hit is verifiable (OQ-DO-28); the N-catch-up test (AC-DO-175) runs for both a `_process`-driven and a stub physics-driven driver. *Why:* PF-41; the one-frame read stops `tv` and `p` straddling a tick, which would flag a false `DEFECT_UNCAUSED` on a tick boundary. *(orchestrator ruling: the qa-lead's "same frame" gap is closed here: one refresh reads both before any other callback, using the Filter's memo.)* *AC group:* pages (truth), classifier, inertness (a second `perceived` call hits the memo).

**DO-21. The discriminator `desync_classifier`.** *Rule:* The model classifies each listed `(ref, viewer)` at each class fold (DO-21 (5)) by a pure function (formula in Formulas; proposed registry name `desync_classifier`); class table below. Evaluation order is fixed and the **first match wins**; `UNAVAILABLE` is first and `DEFECT_UNCAUSED` is the explicit catch-all, so no input falls through. **Honest means exact equality** `p_int == tv`, where `p_int` is `p.displayed_value` itself on the honest path (`motion_mode == HONEST`; pf:247: the honest path is exact by rule and the value is an integer in the owner's unit by the Filter's contract) and an integer computed from the integer `displayed_pm` in every other mode (F-1) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. **`ledger_active(ref, viewer)`** (the Overlay's only reading of the Sanity ledger; it replaces every earlier "has a ledger entry"): true iff `sanity.ledger.lies[]` holds an entry for `viewer` whose `subject_ref` is the subject of the ref's published `lie_targets(ref)` record, whose `params` name that record's surface or quantity (or the subject has exactly one ref), and whose `state` is **`ACTIVE`** and which is **listed for the viewer** (`ledger_listed(player_id, lie_id)`, Sanity's diagnostic accessor, SN-23: every `ACTIVE` row of the seat is listed, whether the seat is in the subject's compartment or the entry is `away`; the Overlay never reads `sanity.perception`) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*. *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix: an `ACTIVE` row of a compartment the seat has left stays listed with `away` true and keeps its clock; the Filter keeps its entry and plays no return on leaving or on coming back (SN-23, EC-SN-35), so such a row is live exactly like one the seat stands beside and the class does not change; E-DO-53, AC-DO-194.)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the earlier `subject_of(ref)` was never defined. If the subject were the whole compartment, a lie on `T` would make `ledger_active` true for the honest `P` and `p_o2` refs and `n_skew` would raise a false defect through row 4 branch (b); the same holds for a partial lie on a multi-surface node. The join is therefore scoped to the ref's own surface or quantity through a predicate the Filter and Sanity publish, never guessed. See paragraph (5).)* A Sanity row in `PENDING` (onset delay `ONSET_DELAY_RUNS` 10 to 25 runs, 2 to 5 s, not yet in `sanity.perception`) and the ended states `EXPIRED`, `DEBUNKED` and `INVALIDATED` are **not** active. With two `ACTIVE` entries on one subject the Overlay matches the lowest `(start_run, lie_id)`, the Filter's own pick (PF-46, pf:467); Sanity mints `lie_id` per seat at the accept and publishes it in the ledger row and in `sanity.perception` (sn:170, sn:345), so the two picks agree on a `start_run` tie, and the row shows `lie_collisions` and a display-only `paired` mark (ledger `lie_id` equals `Perceived.lie_id`). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* The join is by `(viewer, subject_ref, surface or quantity)`, **never by subject alone and never by `lie_id`** (`lie_id` is used only for the tie-break above, the `paired` mark and the `ledger_listed` membership query, never to match a row to a ref; the ledger row has carried `lie_id` since Sanity's 2026-10-27 re-review, sn:170, sn:345, and OQ-DO-1 is closed) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. The tolerance `tol(kind)` is used only for the `RETURNING` and `DEFECT_SILENT_LIE` tests and is **the owner's own dirty-key quantum for that field**, cited as such and never as a display quantum: Graph LS-20 (lsg:201): `T` 1000 mC, `P` 10 dkPa, `p_o2` 5 dkPa, store 20 permille, intensity 100 permille, `hp` 250 bp; Suit vitals buckets (suit-and-equipment.md:270-277 (SE-38)): reserve and cell 5 percent of maximum, `integrity_bp` 250 bp; binary-frame refs (`STATE_GLYPH`, `HOUSING_GLOW`, `MACHINE_FRAME`, and any ref carrying a `SPRITE_SWAP` lie, including a `FALSE_OK` or `FALSE_FAIL` drawn on a housing appearance frame, pf:82, PF-19) compare as 0 or 1 with `tol` 0. `FALSE_OK` and `FALSE_FAIL` on a needle dial or digital readout are continuous zone-targeting lies (Filter PF-13, D.3 `target_pm`; STALE readout, PF-18): they use the owner's quantum, `tol_eff` and the `GLIDE_REACH_FRAC` reach age exactly like `GLIDE`. `LEAK_EPS_DEG` 0.25 applies **only** to the optional drawn-versus-perceived probe (DO-22), which compares in needle-angle space. No tolerance value is invented. Fields for which the owner publishes no quantum (`o2_pm`, `n_o2`, `n_inert`) use `tol` 0 until the Graph owner lists one (requested, OQ-DO-3). **Effective tolerance:** a lying `displayed_value` is quantised to one per mille of the instrument range, because `displayed_value` derives from the integer `displayed_pm` (PF-8, pf:670), so a `tol` of 0 would let rounding jitter fail an honest return. The classifier therefore uses `tol_eff(ref) = max(tol(kind), q_display(ref))`, with `q_display = (value_hi - value_lo) / 1000` from the Filter's PF-45 calibration record (published on the Filter's dev channel, registry `filter_counters`, OQ-DO-2 (ii) resolved 2026-10-27; **for a ref whose calibration is unavailable, kinds with `tol` 0 skip the step sub-test of `RETURNING` and are bounded only by `t_return_max`**). `HONEST` stays exact equality. Boundary rule: `disc == tol_eff` is *inside* the tolerance. *Why:* Pillar 1 (an uncaused difference is always a defect) and Pillar 4 (a lie with no leak is a defect). *Reconciliations, each an orchestrator ruling:* **(1) `RETURNING` is a wander-safe closure test bounded by `t_return_max`, never by `LEAK_FADE_S`.** The first-draft ruling bounded `RETURNING` by `LEAK_FADE_S` 1.5 s and `|disc| <= tol`. The Filter's return is a rate-limited follower at `GLIDE_RETURN_RATE` that tracks a live true value and takes `t_return = disc_end / (rm_ret x GLIDE_RETURN_RATE)` for a static true value (`rm_ret = max(rm, RM_RETURN_FLOOR)`, `RM_RETURN_FLOOR` 0.83, the Filter's D.6 as amended 2026-10-27), at most `D_MAX_FRAC_ARC x arc_deg / GLIDE_RETURN_RATE` = 96 / 3 = 32 s at `rm` 1.0 and 38.4 s at `rm` 0.6 (`rm` is latched per lie, E-PF-43; the Filter's own example, pf D.6, is a 60-deg discrepancy: 20 s at 3.0 deg/s and 24.0 s under reduced motion). The Filter publishes `t_return_max` as its `t_clear`, never less than `WANDER_BLEND_S`. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* `LEAK_FADE_S` bounds only the amplitude fade when `disc_end <= 2 x LEAK_EPS_DEG` (pf:731). Read literally, a `LEAK_FADE_S` bound would flag the first 18 s of an honest return as `DEFECT_UNCAUSED`. The Filter's locked behaviour wins: `RETURNING` holds iff the **stall clock** `t_stall <= t_return_max(lie)`, **published by the Filter per lie on its dev channel (registry `filter_counters`)** (it depends on `rm`, so it is never a constant of this GDD), and the discrepancy behaves as a return. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the bound used to run on the total time since `RETURN` began, which assumes a static true value. The Filter's follower tracks a live true value, so a degrading node can legitimately keep a return open far longer than 20 s, and the old test raised `DEFECT_UNCAUSED` against a correct Filter. The clock now measures only time without progress: `T_stall` is re-anchored at every class fold on which the displayed value moved toward the true value by at least `q_display`, so the bound catches a stalled follower and never a long honest one. A windowed progress floor catches a follower that creeps or hobbles without ever re-anchoring.)* Because the Filter's wander term keeps running for `WANDER_BLEND_S` (3 s, cited) into `RETURN` (`w_ret_pm = w_pm_end_shape x (1 - smoothstep(0, WANDER_BLEND_S, t_ret))` fades out with the return, pf D.6, and is exactly 0 from `t_ret` = `WANDER_BLEND_S`, which is what lets the step slack be the display quantum), a per-step monotone test would false-flag an honest return, so the test has two phases: for `t_ret < WANDER_BLEND_S` the **envelope** `disc_t <= disc_end + abs(tv_t - tv_end) + tol_eff`; for `t_ret >= WANDER_BLEND_S` the **step test** `disc_t <= disc_prev + abs(tv_t - tv_prev) + q_display` at class-fold cadence (the second term lets a moving true value be tracked: the follower is monotone toward it and cannot overshoot; the slack is the display quantum `q_display`, no longer the tolerance, which was about 20 times the legitimate per-fold motion and let a needle diverging at tens of degrees per second read `RETURNING`) plus the **windowed progress floor** of F-1. **Binary-frame refs** (`SPRITE_SWAP`, `STATE_GLYPH`, `HOUSING_GLOW`, `MACHINE_FRAME`) cut back at removal (pf:778), so a non-zero `disc` on one of them in `RETURN` is `DEFECT_UNCAUSED` (the gleam decay is not a value discrepancy). `LEAK_FADE_S` is not restated with any other value. Formula, variables and three worked returns are in Formulas (`desync_classifier`). **(2) Two classes are added** to the first-draft list: `SKEW` and `DEFECT_BYPASS`. `SKEW` is the lie-presence skew between Sanity and the Filter (Sanity rebuilds `sanity.perception` each run, five runs per second, and the Filter reads it on the next frame); it was drafted as `PENDING` and **renamed `SKEW` at the checkpoint** because `PENDING` is already Sanity's ledger state (sn:388-389) and this GDD's channel status. `DEFECT_BYPASS` is implied by the render-probe ask (DO-22). **(3) Precedence**: the tools-programmer's guarded predicates are kept, with defects ranked above notes so that no `NOTE` can shadow a `DEFECT` when two classes overlap, with exactly two by-design exceptions that rank first: `UNAVAILABLE` (an input is missing, so no defect can be asserted) and `SKEW` (bounded by `DIAG_SKEW_GRACE_TICKS`, so it cannot hide a defect for longer than a few Sanity runs). The qa-lead's alternative order, `UNAVAILABLE` > `DEFECT_NO_LEAK` > `DEFECT_SILENT_LIE` > `DELIBERATE` > `RETURNING` > `HONEST` > `DEFECT_UNCAUSED`, agrees on the defect-first principle and omits only the two added classes. **(4) `lie_id` during `RETURN` is not assumed.** The Filter says the entry is absent in `RETURN` (pf:502: "`RETURN`: entry absent and discrepancy at or above `LEAK_EPS_DEG`") and the Filter has confirmed that `lie_id` is kept through `RETURN` and is 0 once the follower closes (registry `filter_counters`; OQ-DO-2 (v) resolved 2026-10-27). The classifier is independent of the answer: `SKEW` counts `lie_id != 0` only while `motion_mode != RETURN`, and `RETURNING` tests `motion_mode == RETURN` and not `ledger_active` with no `lie_id` test. **(5) Folds, intent, tags and the join** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. (i) **Folds.** The model classifies at a **class fold**: at most once per change of Sanity's `run` (`T` crossing a multiple of `SANITY_DRIFT_DIVISOR` 12), on the first Overlay `_process` callback after the change, independent of `DIAG_REFRESH_HZ`, of the refresh throttle (F-7), of the rendered frame rate and of `Engine.time_scale`; the numerals only display the latest fold. Every counter (`n_eq`, `n_skew`, `n_leak`, the stall clock, the progress window) is **tick-denominated** and advances at a fold by `dT = T_fold - T_prev_fold` only when `T` has changed, so a paused kernel (`T` frozen) cannot accrue grace and a 20, 60 or 144 fps session reaches the same class at the same `T`. (ii) **Intent.** The Filter publishes, per `(ref, viewer)` on its dev channel, a lie-intent record `lie_intent` `{d_intended_pm, short_reach, not_drawn}`: `d_intended_pm` is the discrepancy the Filter intends at this fold before slew and wander (per mille of the instrument range: GLIDE `min(delta_pm, D_eff x 1000 / arc_deg)`, FALSE_OK or FALSE_FAIL `min(delta_pm, |u_zone - u_true|)`, so it tracks a sweeping true value and reaches 0 when the true value reaches an absolute target); `short_reach` is true when `D_eff < D_VIS_MIN_DEG` (E-PF-10, including `room_deg <= 0`, E-PF-56); `not_drawn` is `NONE`, `UNRENDERABLE` (no calibration, E-PF-11) or `INELIGIBLE` (needle under the floor, or a sheet missing the opposite honest frame, E-PF-11, E-PF-12). The Overlay converts `d_int_u = round_half_up(d_intended_pm x (value_hi - value_lo) / 1000)`. A needle lie is a **silent lie** only when the Filter *intended* a visible departure (`d_int_u > tol_eff`, `short_reach` false, `not_drawn` NONE) and drew none. Every other absence of departure is the Filter's designed fallback and reads `DELIBERATE` with a tag, in this priority order: `NO_NEEDLE` (`not_drawn` is not NONE; the Filter creates no needle lie and `lie_id` stays 0), `SHORT_REACH`, `SUB_TOL` (`d_int_u <= tol_eff`, which includes the Suit 5-percent boundary where `tol_eff` equals `D_VIS_MIN_DEG`, and a true value sweeping through an absolute target) and `ONSET` (the exact predicate is `onset` in F-1). These are the second tag. The first tag is `LIE` only for a **verified** result; when the Filter has not yet published `lie_intent` (row 4 is suspended and can never raise a defect) or the ref is in `SUBJECT_SCOPE`, the first tag is `UNVERIFIED` instead (paragraph (v)) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*.
