| `start_run`, `active_run`, `end_run`, `run` | int runs | 0 to run length | `sanity.ledger` (sn:170); Sanity's run counter | The ledger's lie window, measured from activation (`active_run`; `start_run` is used only in the `(start_run, lie_id)` tie-break); one run = `SANITY_DRIFT_DIVISOR` 12 ticks = 0.2 s |
| `tol(kind)` | int, owner's unit | 0 to 1000 | The owner's dirty-key quantum: Graph LS-20 (lsg:201) `T` 1000 mC, `P` 10 dkPa, `p_o2` 5 dkPa, store 20 permille, intensity 100 permille, `hp` 250 bp; Suit vitals buckets (suit-and-equipment.md:270-277 (SE-38)) 5 percent of maximum for reserve and cell, `integrity_bp` 250 bp; `o2_pm`, `n_o2`, `n_inert` have none (0) | Used only by rows 4 and 7; never by row 8 |
| `value_lo`, `value_hi` | int, owner's unit | per ref | Filter PF-45 calibration (published by the Filter (registry `filter_counters`) on its dev channel) | Set `q_display`; `displayed_value` is quantised to one per mille of this range |
| `GLIDE_REACH_FRAC` | float | 0.6 `[PLACEHOLDER]`, 0.4 to 0.8 | Perception Filter, cited (pf D.2) | The fraction of a lie's life by which its departure has completed |
| `SWAP_LEAD_S`, `WANDER_BLEND_S` | float s | 3 and 3 `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Perception Filter, cited: `SWAP_LEAD_S` (pf:1214), `WANDER_BLEND_S` (pf:1186) | The binary-frame gleam lead; the wander fade-out into `RETURN` |
| `t_return_max(lie)` | float s | 0 to the Filter's bound (32 s at `rm` 1.0; 38.4 s at `rm` 0.6, for the 96-deg cap) | **Published by the Filter (registry `filter_counters`)** per lie; depends on `rm`, latched per lie (E-PF-43) | The longest an honest return may take; never a constant of this GDD |
| `disc_end`, `tv_end`, `disc_prev`, `tv_prev` | int, owner's unit | per ref | Latched by the model on the first `RETURN` fold and at each class fold | The envelope anchor and the step reference *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| `p_int` | int, owner's unit | the instrument range | model | `p.displayed_value` itself when `motion_mode == HONEST` (an integer by the Filter contract); otherwise `floor_div(value_lo x 1000 + span x displayed_pm + 500, 1000)` from the integer `displayed_pm`, half rounding up and negative values rounding toward minus infinity (GDScript integer `/` truncates toward zero, so `floor_div` is explicit) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `disc` | int, owner's unit | 0 to the instrument range (0 or 1 for binary-frame refs) | model | `abs(p_int - tv)` |
| `q_display` | int, owner's unit | >= 1 | model, from the Filter PF-45 calibration (published by the Filter, registry `filter_counters`) | `ceil((value_hi - value_lo) / 1000)`; the display quantum |
| `tol_eff` | int, owner's unit | >= `tol(kind)` | model | `max(tol(kind), q_display)`; `tol(kind)` when `q_display` is unpublished |
| `step_enabled` | bool | true / false | model | `q_display` is published; false skips the step sub-test and the progress floor of `closing(t)` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `lie_age` | int runs | 0 to unbounded (the ledger window is `end_run - active_run`) | model | `run - active_run`; one run = 12 ticks = 0.2 s |
| `reach_age` | int runs | 0 to 0.8 x the lie life | model | `ceil_div(GLIDE_REACH_FRAC_pm x (end_run - active_run), 1000)`; binary-frame refs: `SWAP_LEAD_S` x 5 |
| `n_eq`, `n_skew`, `n_leak` | int ticks | 0 to unbounded | model, at class folds | Consecutive-tick counters, advancing by `dT` only when `T` changed: `n_eq` counts `disc <= tol_eff` only from the first fold on which `lie_age >= reach_age` and `silent_ok` hold; `n_skew` counts the ledger / `Perceived` presence disagreement; `n_leak` counts a probe-confirmed missing gleam *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `progress`, `T_stall`, `t_stall` | owner's unit; tick; s | `progress` signed; `t_stall` 0 to `t_return_max` | model | The movement of the displayed value toward the true value per fold; the stall clock is re-anchored whenever `progress >= q_display`, so `t_stall` is the time without progress *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `DIAG_RETURN_WINDOW_TICKS`, `DIAG_RETURN_FLOOR_QD` | int ticks; int | 300 and 5 `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Tuning Knobs | The progress-floor window (5 s) and the floor in display quanta; applied from `WANDER_BLEND_S` plus one window into a return |
| `t_ret` | float s | 0 up | model, kernel ticks / `TICK_RATE` | Time since `RETURN` began (it selects the envelope or the step phase); freezes while the kernel is `PAUSED`. The time bound now runs on `t_stall` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `angle_mdeg(pm)` | int millidegrees | -arc_deg/2 x 1000 .. +arc_deg/2 x 1000 | model, from the instrument's `arc_deg` (240, in-world-instruments-and-inspect-view.md:201) and `value_lo`/`value_hi` (IW-9, :222-227), mapped as IW's `theta(pm)` (:553) | The needle angle a per-mille value maps to, in integer arithmetic; row 5 compares in this space only. A calibration with `value_hi <= value_lo` or `arc_deg <= 0` is `UNAVAILABLE` / `NO_RESOLVER` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `drawn_mdeg`, `probe_present`, `drawn_leak(ref)`, `leak_probe_present` | int millidegrees, bool; int per mille, bool | per instrument | Instruments IW-38 probes (optional; OQ-DO-26) | The **pre-snap** needle angle the node drew this frame, and the gleam amplitude it drew; row 5 and row 3 (iii) only *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `DIAG_SILENT_LIE_GRACE_TICKS`, `DIAG_SKEW_GRACE_TICKS`, `DIAG_NO_LEAK_GRACE_TICKS` | int kernel ticks | 120, 24 and 24 `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Tuning Knobs | Slack after the age gate (2 s); the Sanity/Filter skew allowance (two Sanity runs); the probe-confirmed missing-gleam allowance. Ticks, never rendered frames: a frame count shrinks as the frame rate rises, so at 144 fps a 24-frame grace was shorter than one 12-tick Sanity run *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `LEAK_EPS_DEG`, `LEAK_EPS_MDEG` | float deg; int | 0.25; 250 | registry `lie_leak_amplitude`, cited, **never restated** (`LEAK_EPS_MDEG` is the same value in integer millidegrees) | Used only in row 5 (integer millidegrees, pre-snap) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `AMP_FLOOR_PM` (and `AMP_FLOOR_S3_PM` 250) | int per mille | 400 (stage 0-1); `floor_eff` 400/325/250 at stages 0-1/2/3 | registry `lie_leak_amplitude` and `AMP_FLOOR_PM`, cited | The Filter's stage-indexed gleam amplitude floor; row 3 (iii) reads `floor_eff` for the viewer's stage, only | *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass after design review)*

**Output Range:** exactly one of nine classes per `(ref, viewer)` per class fold: `UNAVAILABLE`, `SKEW`, `DEFECT_NO_LEAK`, `DEFECT_SILENT_LIE`, `DEFECT_BYPASS`, `DELIBERATE`, `RETURNING`, `HONEST`, `DEFECT_UNCAUSED`, with severities `NOTE`, `NOTE`, `DEFECT`, `DEFECT`, `DEFECT`, `NOTE` (first tag `LIE` when verified, `UNVERIFIED` when not, plus a second tag), `NOTE`, `OK`, `DEFECT` (with a `why`). Row 1 is first and row 9 is the explicit catch-all, so the function is total: every input combination, including an active ledger row with `lie_id` 0, a `RETURN` that fails its test and a Filter that has not published `lie_intent`, maps to one class. Boundary rules: `disc == tol_eff` is **inside** the tolerance (the silent-lie counter keeps running, the envelope passes); the step slack is exactly `q_display`, inclusive; `HONEST` is **exact** equality of the integers `p_int` and `tv`; `t_stall == t_return_max` is inside the bound; `d_int_u == tol_eff` is inside the tolerance (`SUB_TOL`, not a silent lie); the probe check at exactly `LEAK_EPS_MDEG` passes. Nothing about the function is hashed or replicated. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

**Example (Graph `hp`, `tol` 250 bp, `span` 10000 so `q_display` = ceil(10000 / 1000) = 10 bp and `tol_eff` = 250, `HP_FAIL` 3000; `p_int` = 10 x `displayed_pm`)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: re-based on grid-valid values, the counters in ticks, the intent record added)*: the table is the fixture set, one row per class, plus the `SKEW` boundary, the Sanity `PENDING` case, the `UNAVAILABLE` reasons and the designed-fallback tags. The `hp` dial is a synthetic fixture: Instruments holds `strain` (hp) out of the MVP manifest (in-world-instruments-and-inspect-view.md:227), so the MVP live refs are `p_o2`, `P`, `T`, `stock`, `load`:

| # | Setup | Inputs | Class |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Honest compartment `P` | `tv` 1013 dkPa, displayed 1013 (`motion_mode` HONEST), `lie_id` 0, not `ledger_active` | `HONEST` OK |
| 2 | Exactness | `tv` 21000 mC, displayed 21001 (1 mC off, far inside `tol` 1000), HONEST mode, `lie_id` 0, on the second consecutive fold (`n_diff` 2; the first fold reads `UNAVAILABLE` / `MEMO_UNCONFIRMED`) | `DEFECT_UNCAUSED` DEFECT, `why` `NO_LEDGER_DIFF` (proves row 8 is exact equality, not `tol`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| 3 | `FALSE_OK` on a failed node, onset | `tv` hp 2400, `displayed_pm` 265 (`p_int` 2650, `disc` 250, inside `tol_eff`), `ledger_active`, `lie_id` 7, GLEAM + GLIDE (3), `lie_age` 40 runs < `reach_age` 120, `d_int_u` 2500 | `DELIBERATE` NOTE `LIE`, tagged `ONSET` |
| 4 | Same lie, established | `tv` 2400, `displayed_pm` 490 (`p_int` 4900, `disc` 2500), `lie_id` 7, `leak_modes` 3 | `DELIBERATE` NOTE `LIE` |
| 5 | Same lie, leak dropped | as row 4 with either `leak_modes` 2 (GLIDE only, the tripwire) or `drawn_leak(ref)` 0 against `leak_amplitude_pm` 700 (at or above `floor_eff` 400, stage 0-1) for `n_leak` 36 > 24 ticks, or `leak_repaired` rising with the Filter naming this ref's `lie_id` (an unnamed rise is one page-level `UNATTRIBUTED` event, not this class) | `DEFECT_NO_LEAK` DEFECT | *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass after design review)*
| 6 | Lie that never renders | `tv` 2400, `displayed_pm` 240 (`p_int` 2400), `lie_id` 7, `ledger_active`, GLEAM set, `lie_age` 130 runs >= `reach_age` 120, `d_int_u` 2500 > `tol_eff` 250, `not_drawn` NONE, `n_eq` 132 ticks > 120 (the first reachable value past the grace; 121 is a predicate probe) | `DEFECT_SILENT_LIE` DEFECT *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| 7 | Uncaused difference | `tv` 1013, displayed 1010 dkPa (HONEST mode), `lie_id` 0, not `ledger_active`, `n_diff` 2 | `DEFECT_UNCAUSED` DEFECT, `why` `NO_LEDGER_DIFF` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| 8 | Sanity/Filter skew | ledger row `ACTIVE` this run, `Perceived.lie_id` still 0, `not_drawn` NONE, `n_skew` 12 ticks <= 24 | `SKEW` NOTE |
| 9 | Skew that does not clear | as row 8 with `n_skew` 36 ticks > 24 | `DEFECT_SILENT_LIE` DEFECT (branch b: the Filter never picked the entry up) |
| 10 | Sanity `PENDING` row | the ledger row is `PENDING` (onset delay), `lie_id` 0, `tv` = displayed | `HONEST` OK (a `PENDING` row is not `ledger_active`; no lie exists yet) |
| 11 | Remote seat | viewer 2 on a one-seat build, or any viewer on a `MIRROR` | `UNAVAILABLE` / `REMOTE_VIEWER` or `NO_LEDGER`, NOTE |
| 12 | Probe present, bypass | the drawn pre-snap angle differs from the angle implied by `displayed_pm` by 300 millidegrees > `LEAK_EPS_MDEG` 250 | `DEFECT_BYPASS` DEFECT (at exactly 250 the probe check passes: inclusive) |
| 13 | Dial with less than the minimum room | `short_reach` true (room 8 deg < `D_VIS_MIN_DEG` 12), `displayed_pm` 240 = `tv`, `ledger_active`, `lie_id` 7, GLEAM, `lie_age` 150 >= 120, `n_eq` would be 300 | `DELIBERATE` NOTE `LIE`, tagged `SHORT_REACH`, never `DEFECT_SILENT_LIE` |
| 14 | No room at all | `room_deg` <= 0, `d_intended_pm` 0 (`d_int_u` 0), `short_reach` true, otherwise as row 13 | `DELIBERATE` tagged `SHORT_REACH` |
| 15 | The Suit boundary | a Suit dial whose `tol_eff` equals `D_VIS_MIN_DEG` (12 deg = 50 per mille of the 240-deg arc), `d_int_u` == `tol_eff`, `disc` 0, `n_eq` would be 300 | `DELIBERATE` tagged `SUB_TOL` (equality is inside the tolerance) |
| 16 | Ineligible needle | `not_drawn` INELIGIBLE, `ledger_active`, `lie_id` 0, `n_skew` 240 ticks | `DELIBERATE` tagged `NO_NEEDLE`, never branch (b) |
| 17 | True value sweeping through an absolute target | a `FALSE_OK` toward `u_zone` 900 per mille while `tv` sweeps up through it at 50, 100 or 250 bp/s; the Filter's `d_intended_pm` falls through 0 (`d_int_u` <= `tol_eff`) while `disc` <= `tol_eff` | `DELIBERATE` tagged `SUB_TOL`; `n_eq` is 0 at every fold in which `d_int_u <= tol_eff` |
| 18 | `lie_intent` unpublished | `ledger_active`, `lie_id` 7, GLEAM, `disc` 0, `lie_age` 150, no `lie_intent` | `DELIBERATE` tagged `UNVERIFIED` (why `INTENT_UNKNOWN`; row 4 suspended), never `LIE` and never a defect; the unverified count is 1 and the coverage readout shows `intent 0/N` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| 19 | Honest closed return | `motion_mode` RETURN, `displayed_pm` 240 (`p_int` 2400), `tv` 2400, not `ledger_active`, `lie_id` 7 | `HONEST` OK (a closed return; row 7 excludes `disc` 0) |
| 20 | Honest off-grid true value | `motion_mode` HONEST, displayed 2405 = `tv` 2405 (not a multiple of 10), `lie_id` 0 | `HONEST` OK (`p_int` is the honest `displayed_value`, never rounded onto the 10-bp grid) |
| 21 | T-only lie on a three-quantity compartment | compartment `c2`, refs `T`, `P`, `p_o2`; one `ACTIVE` ledger row on `c2` naming quantity `T` | `T`: `DELIBERATE` (or per its inputs); `P` and `p_o2`: `HONEST`, `n_skew` 0 |
| 22 | One-surface lie on a three-surface node | a node with three surfaces; one `ACTIVE` row naming surface 2 | surface 2 per its inputs; surfaces 1 and 3: `HONEST` |
| 23 | Subject-granularity fallback | as row 21 with `lie_targets` unpublished for `c2` and exactly one live row (`ACTIVE` and listed) on it | `T`, `P` and `p_o2` all `DELIBERATE` tagged `UNVERIFIED` (why `SUBJECT_SCOPE`); none `HONEST`, `n_eq` and `n_skew` 0 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| 24 | Binary-frame honest | a `STATE_GLYPH` ref, `tv` 1, displayed frame state 1, `lie_id` 0, not `ledger_active`, no calibration published | `HONEST` OK: `tol_eff` 0, no span, arc or `q_display` read, not `UNAVAILABLE` / `NO_RESOLVER` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| 25 | Binary-frame fair lie with gleam | a `HOUSING_GLOW` `FALSE_OK`, `tv` 0, displayed frame state 1 (`disc` 1), `ledger_active`, `lie_id` 7, GLEAM set, `lie_age` 20 >= gate 15 runs, `lie_intent` published, `not_drawn` NONE | `DELIBERATE` NOTE `LIE` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| 26 | Binary-frame swap never drawn | as row 25 with displayed frame state 0 (`disc` 0), `n_eq` 132 ticks > 120 | `DEFECT_SILENT_LIE` DEFECT *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| 27 | Binary-frame missing gleam | as row 25 with `leak_modes` 2 (GLEAM clear) | `DEFECT_NO_LEAK` DEFECT *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| 28 | End of a lie, `lie_targets` unpublished | compartment `c2` (refs `T`, `P`, `p_o2`), one `ACTIVE` listed row on `c2` that leaves `ACTIVE` (`EXPIRED`) at run r; zero live rows, so `scope` is `RESOLVED` (vacuous); the Filter keeps `T`'s record (`lie_id` 7, `GLIDE`, `disc` 3000 mC) for two folds; `n_skew` 12, then 24, then (if the Filter never picks up the end) 36 | `T`: `SKEW` at 12 and 24 ticks; with the Filter's `RETURN` at fold r + 2 `RETURNING` (`n_skew` 0); at 36 ticks (> 24) `DEFECT_UNCAUSED`, `why` `LIE_WITHOUT_LEDGER`; `P` and `p_o2`: `HONEST`, `n_skew` 0 at every fold *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* |
| 29 | The seat leaves the compartment of a live lie | as row 4 (the established `hp` lie), the seat moves to another compartment: the ledger row stays `ACTIVE`, `away` true, `ledger_listed` true (SN-23); the Filter keeps its entry | `DELIBERATE` NOTE `LIE`, unchanged at every fold: no `SKEW`, no `RETURNING`, no defect, on leaving and on coming back *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* |

**Worked example: three returns** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: re-based on grid-valid sequences; the earlier fixtures used values such as 4162.5 and 4787 that the integer `displayed_pm` cannot produce)* (Graph `hp` 0 to 10000 bp, so one per mille is 10 bp and `q_display` is 10; needle `arc_deg` 240 (Instruments IW rule at in-world-instruments-and-inspect-view.md:201; also the Filter's own example at pf:648), so 1 deg = 41.67 bp; the Filter's `GLIDE_RETURN_RATE` 3.0 deg/s = 125 bp/s, which is 25 bp per class fold of 12 ticks (0.2 s), and 104 bp/s at `rm` 0.6 (`rm_ret` 0.83, 2.5 deg/s); a 60-deg discrepancy at removal is 2500 bp, which the Filter's D.6 example gives as 20 s, and 24.0 s under reduced motion; `p_int` = 10 x `displayed_pm`):

1. *Honest return, static true value.* `tv` 2400 held, lie removed with `displayed_pm` 490 (4900, `disc_end` 2500). At `t_ret` 1.0 s (inside `WANDER_BLEND_S`) `displayed_pm` is 479 (4790: the follower's 4775 plus a wander residue, on the grid), so `disc_t` = 2390; envelope test: 2390 <= 2500 + 0 + 250 = 2750 holds. (The Filter's wander term fades out over `WANDER_BLEND_S` and can move the displayed value by more than the follower's rate alone, so a per-step test would be unsafe in this window; the envelope is anchored on the removal instead.) At `t_ret` 6.0 s (past `WANDER_BLEND_S`, so the step test applies) `displayed_pm` is 415 (4150, `disc_t` 1750); at the previous fold (5.8 s) it was 417 (4170, `disc_prev` 1770): 1750 <= 1770 + 0 + 10 holds, and `progress` = sign(2400 - 4170) x (4150 - 4170) = +20 >= `q_display` 10, so `T_stall` is re-anchored to 6.0 s. The windowed floor (applied from `t_ret` 8.0 s) needs the last 5 s to have closed at least 5 x 10 = 50 bp; they closed about 625. `RETURNING` NOTE throughout; at `t_ret` 20.0 s `displayed_pm` is 240, `p_int` 2400 = `tv`, and the class is `HONEST` (row 8). The same lie at `rm` 0.6 takes 24.0 s, inside the Filter's published `t_return_max` for that lie (and the stall clock is re-anchored every fold anyway).
2. *Moving true value.* The node degrades: `tv` falls from 7500 at 200 bp/s (40 bp per fold) until it reaches 0 at 37.5 s, while the displayed value starts at `displayed_pm` 1000 (10000, `disc_end` 2500) and the follower closes at 125 bp/s (25 bp per fold). The discrepancy **grows** by 15 bp per fold until `tv` stops, and the follower, then near 5310, needs about 42 s more: the whole return lasts about 80 s, far past the 20 s `t_return_max` of a static target. Step test at each fold: `disc_t` = `disc_prev` + 15 <= `disc_prev` + abs(`tv_t` - `tv_prev`) (40) + `q_display` (10) holds; `progress` is +25 >= 10 at every fold, so `T_stall` is re-anchored at every fold and `t_stall` never exceeds 0.2 s; the floor sees about 625 bp per 5 s. `RETURNING` (tagged `TARGET_MOVING` while `tv` moves) at every fold for the whole 80 s, then `HONEST`. (The earlier bound on the total time since `RETURN` began raised `DEFECT_UNCAUSED` here at 20 s against a correct Filter.) A step test without the `abs(tv_t - tv_prev)` term would flag it at the first fold.
3. *Stuck return.* The displayed value freezes at 4150 (`disc` 1750, static `tv`) at `t_ret` 6.0 s and stays. The step test passes (a frozen discrepancy does not grow), but `progress` is 0 at every fold, so `T_stall` stays at 6.0 s, and the **windowed progress floor** catches it: from `t_ret` 10.8 s the last 5 s have closed less than 50 bp, the row fails and the class becomes `DEFECT_UNCAUSED` (`RETURN_STALLED`), within one window of the stall. If the floor were disabled, the **stall bound** would catch it when `t_stall` passes `t_return_max(lie)` (at `t_ret` 38.0 s for the 32 s cap at `rm` 1.0). A *creeping* return that moves 5 bp per fold (25 bp/s, above the floor and below `q_display`) never re-anchors and is caught by the stall bound alone. A return whose `disc` grows beyond `disc_prev` + `abs(Delta tv)` + 10 fails at once (`RETURN_GROWTH`): a needle diverging at 40 deg/s (1667 bp/s, 333 bp per fold) fails at the first fold, within 0.2 s. A binary-frame ref (a cracked-housing `HOUSING_GLOW`) in `RETURN` with `disc` 1 fails at once (`BINARY_RETURN`): the frame cut back at removal (pf:778).

**Worked example: silent-lie timing in ticks.** A 40 s lie: `end_run - active_run` = 200 runs (Sanity runs at 5 per second); `reach_age` = ceil(600 x 200 / 1000) = 120 runs = 24 s. A lie that is not rendered although the Filter intended a visible departure (`d_int_u` 2500 > `tol_eff` 250) reads `DELIBERATE` tagged `ONSET` for the first 24 s, however many folds `disc <= tol_eff`; from 24 s the `n_eq` counter (zero until then) advances 12 ticks per fold and must exceed `DIAG_SILENT_LIE_GRACE_TICKS` 120 (2 s of kernel time, 11 folds) before `DEFECT_SILENT_LIE` fires, at about 26.2 s, at 20, 60 or 144 fps alike. A slow `GLIDE` onset never produces a false defect: by the reach age the Filter guarantees arrival at `D_eff >= D_VIS_MIN_DEG` (pf:646); an in-contract onset at 0.5 deg/s (20.8 bp/s) leaves `tol_eff` 250 bp (6 deg) at 12 s (run 60) and reaches 12 deg = 500 bp at the reach age, so it never counts `n_eq`. A lie the Filter designed to draw no needle departure (no room, an intended departure inside `tol_eff`, an ineligible needle) fails `silent_ok`, so `n_eq` stays 0 and the row reads `DELIBERATE` with its tag indefinitely. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*

### F-2. `graph_hook_tick`

The `graph_hook_tick` formula is defined as:

`graph_hook_tick(T) = sum of t_hook(h, T) over the Graph hooks h with hook_runs_this_tick(h, T)`, where `hook_runs_this_tick(h, T) = (T mod divisor(h)) == offset(h)` (kernel CR-3)

The Graph's budget (registry `GRAPH_HOOK_BUDGET_MS`) is a P99 of **the sum of the Graph hooks that run on one tick** while the kernel publishes only per-hook `t_hook`, so the Overlay folds the hooks that ran on a tick into one sample for that tick. The Graph hooks have divisor 6 and offsets 0, 2 and 4 (Graph LS-8) and are chained by `after:` with `tolerates_stale` edges; each runs on its own tick (6n: `graph.propagate.power`, 6n + 2: `graph.propagate.atmo`, 6n + 4: `graph.propagate.failure`), so each hook tick is one sample. **A sample is every tick on which at least one Graph hook ran**, keyed `(T, restore epoch)`: a tick with no Graph hook, a pause or a restore straddle, or a duplicate delivery of the same `(T, epoch)` is dropped and counted, never sampled, so a duplicate cannot enter the window twice *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix: replaces the earlier per-run sum of three hooks, which assumed all three on one tick; the registered formula name changes to `graph_hook_tick`)*.

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `t_hook(h, T)` | int microseconds | 0 to unbounded | The kernel's per-hook `t_hook` for hook `h` on tick `T`, from the kernel's diagnostics timer (the one allow-listed wall-clock reader, H9, kern:781); the Overlay reads no clock of its own | One hook's time on tick `T` |
| `h` | hook id | exactly `graph.propagate.power`, `graph.propagate.atmo`, `graph.propagate.failure` | kernel resolved order (kern:607) | The bare string `graph.propagate` never appears |

**Output Range:** `graph_hook_tick` >= 0 us per hook tick; displayed in ms to two decimals. Never `DEFECT`: the figure is a dev-template number and the budget it is compared with is defined on the Release export template (DO-19). **Example:** at the baseline stagger one step yields three samples: 120 us on the power tick (6n), 150 us on the atmo tick (6n + 2) and 40 us on the failure tick (6n + 4), i.e. 0.12, 0.15 and 0.04 ms; the 310 us total is never a sample. If a configuration ever co-scheduled two Graph hooks on one tick, that tick's sample would be their sum *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*. `step_over_budget` (kern D.6's own example, 1.2 + 0.9 + 0.4 + 0.3 + 0.1 = 2.9 ms against `STEP_BUDGET_MS` 4) is displayed from the kernel, not recomputed.

### F-3. `hook_window_p99`

The `hook_window_p99` formula is defined as:

`hook_window_p99 = S[ ceil_div(99 x n, 100) ]` (recomputed at the class fold, 5 per second, not per hook tick, and its sort cost is inside `n_refs x t_ref_us`'s fold frame budget), where `S` is the ascending sort of the last `n = min(N, samples seen)` values of `graph_hook_tick` (one per hook tick), `N = DIAG_HOOK_WINDOW_RUNS` (nearest-rank percentile, 1-indexed); `verdict = no verdict (dev template) if the template is DEV and DIAG_DEV_TEMPLATE_SCALE_PM == 0; insufficient if n < DIAG_P99_MIN_SAMPLES; WARN if hook_window_p99 > GRAPH_HOOK_BUDGET_MS.P99 or runs_over_max >= DIAG_HOOK_MAX_BREACH_RUNS; NOTE "1 outlier" if runs_over_max == 1; else OK`, where `runs_over_max` is the count of window samples above `GRAPH_HOOK_BUDGET_MS.max` (per hook tick); on the dev template each sample is first divided by the calibration scale (`value x 1000 / DIAG_DEV_TEMPLATE_SCALE_PM`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: complete runs only; one outlier no longer sticks the maximum `WARN` for the whole window; the dev template gets no permanent `WARN`)*

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `N` = `DIAG_HOOK_WINDOW_RUNS` | int hook-tick samples | 100 to 3600 `[PLACEHOLDER]`, default 600 (20 s of samples: 30 hook ticks per second at three per 6-tick step, so 200 steps; name and range unchanged) | Tuning Knobs | The rolling window length, counted in hook-tick samples *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* |
| `n` | int | 0 to `N` | model | Samples currently held (one per hook tick, keyed by `(T, epoch)`); the window advances **per Graph hook tick, not per rendered frame**, and does not advance while the kernel is `PAUSED` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* |
| `DIAG_HOOK_MAX_BREACH_RUNS`, `DIAG_DEV_TEMPLATE_SCALE_PM` | int samples; int per mille | 2 (1 to 10) and 0 (0 = no verdict on the dev template) `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Tuning Knobs | How many hook-tick samples above the max budget make a `WARN`; the dev-to-release timing ratio supplied by the Graph's S1 spike |
| `DIAG_P99_MIN_SAMPLES` | int | 100 to 600 (at most `DIAG_HOOK_WINDOW_RUNS`, asserted at startup and clamped, not only linted) `[PLACEHOLDER]`, default 100 | Tuning Knobs | The smallest `n` that yields a P99 verdict; a P99 over fewer than 100 samples would just be the maximum (100 samples is 33.3 steps, 3.3 s of `RUNNING`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `GRAPH_HOOK_BUDGET_MS` | ms | 0.5 P99 / 1.0 max per hook tick (MVP) = 500 us / 1000 us; 1.0 P99 per tick (VS-A) | registry `GRAPH_HOOK_BUDGET_MS`, **cited verbatim**; defined on the **Release export template**, the sum of the Graph hooks that run on that tick (offsets 0 / 2 / 4) | The budget. The Graph's own row label `GRAPH_HOOK_BUDGET_US` (lsg:724) is never surfaced as a second constant |

**Output Range:** `hook_window_p99` >= 0 us, one of the window's own samples (nearest rank never interpolates); `verdict` in exactly five outcomes {`OK`, `WARN`, `NOTE` "1 outlier", `insufficient samples (n/N)`, `no verdict (dev template)`} *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; **never `DEFECT`** and always labelled "dev template: indicative" (the gate is the Graph's S1 spike and performance gate). The VS-A column of the registry (1.0 ms P99 **per tick**) is a different aggregation (per tick, not per hook tick); it is a contract for later, and the MVP verdict uses the MVP column only. **Example:** `N` 600; hook-tick sample values (illustrative, any mix of the three hooks): 590 at 310 us, 6 at 420 us, 3 at 710 us, 1 at 1120 us. Rank = ceil_div(99 x 600, 100) = 594; sorted positions 1 to 590 are 310, 591 to 596 are 420, 597 to 599 are 710, 600 is 1120, so `S[594]` = 420 us <= 500 us (P99 `OK`); `runs_over_max` = 1 (only the 1120 us run exceeds 1000) < `DIAG_HOOK_MAX_BREACH_RUNS` 2, so the maximum row is `NOTE` "1 outlier: 1120 us", not `WARN` (with two runs above 1000 it would be `WARN`); on the dev template the same figures read "dev template: indicative, no verdict". With `n` 99 the verdict is `insufficient samples (99/100)`, never a number and never `OK`.

### F-4. `dirty_load_pm`

The `dirty_load_pm` formula is defined as:

`dirty_load_pm(b, T) = floor(1000 x keys(b, T) / MAX_DIRTY_KEYS)`; an `ALL` collapse sets `collapse(b, T) = true` and `dirty_load_pm = 1000`

A cascade that approaches `MAX_DIRTY_KEYS` collapses to `ALL` (kern:106, D.9), so the Overlay shows how close each block comes per delivered batch (per tick in a normal frame; in a catch-up frame, one merged batch with the union of the frame's keys, kernel R-12 ratified 2026-10-27) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. The refresh folds the batches since the last refresh into `last`, `peak` (max over the folded ticks) and `collapse_count` per block (DO-18).

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `keys(b, T)` | int | 0 to 256, or `ALL` | The P5 notification batch `(block_id, tick, change_keys)` (CR-10) | Change keys for block `b` at tick `T` (per delivered batch: in a catch-up frame `T` is the frame's last tick and the keys are the union over the frame) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* |
| `MAX_DIRTY_KEYS` | int | 256 | registry `MAX_DIRTY_KEYS`, **never altered** | The collapse threshold |
| `DIAG_DIRTY_WARN_PM` | int per mille | 250 to 900 `[PLACEHOLDER]`, default 500 | Tuning Knobs | `WARN` at or above (inclusive) |
| `DIAG_DIRTY_ALERT_PM` | int per mille | `DIAG_DIRTY_WARN_PM` to 1000 `[PLACEHOLDER]`, default 750 | Tuning Knobs | Pins the row to the top of the page; still `WARN`, no new severity |

**Output Range:** 0 to 1000 per mille per delivered `(block, tick)` batch, and an `ALL` flag shown separately; an `ALL` collapse is `WARN`, never `DEFECT` (it is legal kernel behaviour; a restore produces one `ALL` then silence, annotated `post_restore`). **Example:** the Graph's measured worst cascades (lsg:201, LS-20): 38 keys (MVP) -> floor(38000 / 256) = **148** (`OK`); 127 keys -> 496 (`OK`); 128 keys -> **500** (`WARN`, inclusive boundary); 135 keys (VS-A) -> **527** (`WARN`); 192 keys -> **750** (`WARN`, pinned); an `ALL` collapse -> `collapse_count` + 1, `WARN`. **Catch-up example** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*: a 4-tick catch-up frame in which block `B` dirties 150 keys in tick 1 and 150 different keys in tick 3 delivers one merged batch of 300 keys, more than `MAX_DIRTY_KEYS` 256, so it arrives as `ALL`: `dirty_load_pm` 1000, `collapse_count` + 1, `WARN`, although either tick alone would have read floor(150000 / 256) = 585 (`WARN`); with 100 different keys per tick the union is 200 and the row reads floor(200000 / 256) = **781** (`WARN`, pinned) where either tick alone read floor(100000 / 256) = 390 (`OK`); with the same 100 keys in both ticks the union is 100 and the row reads 390 (`OK`).

### F-5. `queue_load_pm`

The `queue_load_pm` formula is defined as:

`queue_load_pm = floor(1000 x depth / QUEUE_CAPACITY)`, and per issuer `queue_load_pm(i) = floor(1000 x depth(i) / PER_ISSUER_CAPACITY)`; `WARN if max over {total, issuers} >= DIAG_QUEUE_WARN_PM`

`queue_load_pm` is the Overlay's name, not the kernel's: the kernel publishes depths and `lag_ticks`; the Overlay derives the load and attaches a threshold (peak-held per refresh like every `DEFECT`- or `WARN`-capable counter, DO-18). The catch-up lag is cited, not re-derived: `lag_ticks` is the kernel's formula (kern:351; `MAX_TICKS_PER_FRAME` 4), compared with `DIAG_LAG_WARN_TICKS`.

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `depth`, `depth(i)` | int commands | 0 to the capacity | Kernel diagnostics (total and per-issuer queue depth) | The current queue occupancy |
| `QUEUE_CAPACITY` | int | 1024 | registry `QUEUE_CAPACITY`, cited | Total queue capacity |
| `PER_ISSUER_CAPACITY` | int | 256 | registry `PER_ISSUER_CAPACITY`, cited | Per-issuer capacity |
| `DIAG_QUEUE_WARN_PM` | int per mille | 250 to 900 `[PLACEHOLDER]`, default 500 | Tuning Knobs | The `WARN` threshold (inclusive); 500 per mille is 512 commands total or 128 per issuer |
| `lag_ticks`, `DIAG_LAG_WARN_TICKS` | int ticks | 0 to unbounded; 1 to 60 `[PLACEHOLDER]`, default 4 | kernel (kern:351); Tuning Knobs | The simulated-time lag; `WARN` at or above the knob |

**Output Range:** 0 to 1000 per mille (the kernel never exceeds capacity: a full queue rejects with `QUEUE_FULL`, which is itself a `WARN` row on the command-log tail). **Example:** 5 commands queued in total -> floor(5000 / 1024) = **4** (`OK`); 512 -> **500** (`WARN`); 1024 -> 1000; one issuer holding 130 of 256 -> floor(130000 / 256) = **507** (`WARN`, worst issuer). Lag, from the kernel's own example: a 1 s hitch gives `lag_ticks` = 60 - 4 = **56** (`WARN`) after the first frame, repaid at 3 per frame.

### F-6. `ticks_per_overlay_refresh`

The `ticks_per_overlay_refresh` formula is defined as:

`ticks_per_overlay_refresh = TICK_RATE / DIAG_REFRESH_HZ`, and `peak = max(sample_i)` over the ticks folded into one refresh

The kernel emits at `TICK_RATE` 60; numerals refresh at `DIAG_REFRESH_HZ`, so several ticks fold into one displayed value. The refresh is triggered by the accumulated frame delta of the engine's process callback, not by a wall-clock read (DO-19; -> ADR candidate). **Scope** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: `DIAG_REFRESH_HZ` and the throttle of F-7 govern the *numeral refresh and drawing only*. The class fold (DO-21 (5)) runs once per Sanity run (`CLASS_FOLD_HZ` = `TICK_RATE` / `SANITY_DRIFT_DIVISOR` = 5) whatever the refresh does, so changing the refresh rate, throttling it or changing `Engine.time_scale` never changes a classification; it only changes how stale a displayed numeral may be.

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `TICK_RATE` | int Hz | 60 | registry `TICK_RATE`, cited | The kernel rate |
| `DIAG_REFRESH_HZ` | int Hz | 2 to 30 `[PLACEHOLDER]`, default 10 | Tuning Knobs | The numeral refresh rate; markers and debug draws update per rendered frame regardless |

**Output Range:** 2 to 30 ticks per refresh (non-integer when `DIAG_REFRESH_HZ` does not divide 60; the quotient is informative and the model folds whatever ticks arrived). If the rendered frame rate is below `DIAG_REFRESH_HZ` the effective refresh is the frame rate. **Example:** 60 / 10 = **6** ticks, equal to the Graph hooks' divisor, so each refresh folds exactly one Graph step (three hook ticks); at 30 Hz it is 2; at 2 Hz it is 30; at 7 Hz it is 8.57 and some refreshes fold two Graph steps *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*, which is why peak-hold is mandatory; at 8 fps and a 10 Hz setting, each frame refreshes and folds 60 / 8 = 7.5 ticks.

### F-7. `overlay_cpu_us`

The `overlay_cpu_us` formula is defined as:

`overlay_cpu_us = (t_copy_us x TICK_RATE + n_refs x t_ref_us x CLASS_FOLD_HZ + (t_model_us + t_view_us) x refresh_hz + t_draw_us x fps) / fps  <=  OVERLAY_CPU_BUDGET_US`, and the worst frame at each ladder rung `worst_frame_us = max(fold_frame, refresh_frame) <= OVERLAY_FRAME_CEILING_US`, where `fold_frame = t_copy_us x N_catch + n_refs x t_ref_us + t_draw_us` and `refresh_frame = t_model_us + t_view_us + t_draw_us` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the view term moved from per rendered frame to per refresh so the ladder can reach it; the fold term is `n_refs x t_ref_us`; catch-up frames multiply `t_copy_us`; the refresh is spread off the fold frame)*

The Overlay must not distort the numbers it displays, so its own per-rendered-frame cost has a ceiling (dev builds and the playtest preset). The terms are the handler copy per tick, the class fold per Sanity run over the classified set, the formatting and the **dirty-driven** view update per numeral refresh, and the per-frame marker and debug-draw cost; a per-frame ceiling guards the spikes a mean hides. **Spreading rule:** when a class fold and a numeral refresh fall on the same rendered frame the refresh is deferred by one rendered frame and never skipped, so no frame carries both, and the worst frame is the larger of the two (fold-fed numerals lag one extra rendered frame, which AC-DO-45 allows for). **Per-rung composition:** at the 10 and 5 Hz rungs a refresh falls due on or next to every fold frame, so the two frame types alternate and the worst frame is `max(fold_frame, refresh_frame)`; at the 2 Hz rung a refresh coincides with a fold only every fifth fold and is deferred then; no rung ever adds the two. The `FULL` worst frame is the dominant case, and `HIDDEN` and the strip draw nothing and collect the observer set only (DO-35), so their worst frame is `fold_frame` with `t_draw_us` 0.

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `t_copy_us` | int us | 0 to about 40 | **The kernel's diagnostics timer, exposed read-only (DO-33c, APPLIED by the kernel 2026-10-27; the accessor name is the kernel's to give)** (the Overlay reads no wall clock of its own, H9) | Handler copy per tick: the P5 handler keeps **counts only** (block id, key count, an `ALL` flag) in a fixed ring, not key lists, so the term is a few stores *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| `N_catch` | int ticks | 1 to `MAX_TICKS_PER_FRAME` 4 | kernel | Ticks stepped on the frame: a catch-up frame multiplies `t_copy_us` (a fold frame is built with `N_catch` 4 in the worst-frame check) |
| `n_refs` | int refs | 0 to `DIAG_FOLD_REFS_MAX` 48 | model | The classified set (DO-20) |
| `t_ref_us` | int us | 0 to about 12 | same | The classification of one ref (the former `t_fold_us` was this times `n_refs`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| `t_model_us`, `t_view_us` | int us | 0 to about 500 each | same | Formatting and the dirty-row view update, **once per numeral refresh** (only rows the model marked dirty are rebuilt) |
| `t_draw_us` | int us | 0 to about 40 | same | World markers and the layer-0 debug draws, per rendered frame; 0 in `HIDDEN` and the strip |
| `refresh_hz` | int Hz | the current ladder rung, at most `DIAG_REFRESH_HZ` and at most `fps` | model | The numeral refresh rate in force |
| `fps` | float | 20 to 240 | engine | Rendered frames per second |
| `OVERLAY_CPU_BUDGET_US` | int us | 250 to 2000 `[PLACEHOLDER]`, default 1000 | Tuning Knobs | The dev-only mean budget (the same scale as `LIGHT_CPU_BUDGET_US` 1000) |
| `OVERLAY_FRAME_CEILING_US` | int us | 1000 to 8000 `[PLACEHOLDER]`, default 2000; **the budget is owned by the technical director (OQ-DO-13)** | Tuning Knobs | The per-frame ceiling on the Overlay's own cost; a frame above it is counted, and the window maximum is shown. It is not inflated to hide a coincident worst frame: the terms are shrunk and spread instead |

**Output Range:** >= 0 us per rendered frame, averaged over `DIAG_SELF_COST_WINDOW_FRAMES`, plus the window maximum. The row is `WARN` when the window mean is above `OVERLAY_CPU_BUDGET_US` **or** any frame in the window exceeds `OVERLAY_FRAME_CEILING_US` (the spike count is shown). On a breach the view throttles its **numeral refresh and drawing** (never the class fold or collection) along one **data-list ladder** `DIAG_REFRESH_LADDER_HZ`, strictly decreasing, first entry `DIAG_REFRESH_HZ`, last entry at least 2 (default {10, 5, 2}): one rung down per breached window. **Recovery uses back-off, not a fixed count** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: a fixed two clean windows limit-cycled, stepping up into the same breach forever)*: one rung up after `W_up` consecutive clean windows, where `W_up` starts at 2 and doubles (2, 4, 8, capped at 8) each time a step-up is followed by a breach within `W_up` windows, and returns to 2 after an 8-window clean run `[PLACEHOLDER]` (E-DO-35). At the last rung a continuing breach leaves the row `WARN` and does nothing more: the budget, not the Overlay, is then wrong, and it is the technical director's (OQ-DO-13). The gate is the **window mean** and the **ceiling**; the P99 is logged for information only; **collection is never skipped**, so a throttled Overlay still sees every tick, and **a throttle never changes a classification** (F-6, DO-21 (5)). Wall-clock acceptance criteria are `[pending spike measurement on RM-DESKTOP / RM-DECK]` (the named reference machines, `project.yaml` `qa.min_spec`, delegated ruling R12, 2026-10-28; measured on the playtest preset's release export, `forward_plus`, vsync on, 120-frame warm-up, with the startup read-back recorded; the developer's machine is indicative only); the operation-count halves (AC-DO-119, AC-DO-193) run now. **Example (illustrative targets, not measurements):** `t_copy_us` 30, `n_refs` 24, `t_ref_us` 8, `t_model_us` 400, `t_view_us` 150, `t_draw_us` 20 at 60 fps and the 10 Hz rung: (30 x 60 + 24 x 8 x 5 + (400 + 150) x 10 + 20 x 60) / 60 = (1800 + 960 + 5500 + 1200) / 60 = 9460 / 60 = **157.7 us** <= 1000 (`OK`); the worst frames are `fold_frame` = 30 x 4 + 24 x 8 + 20 = **332 us** and `refresh_frame` = 400 + 150 + 20 = **570 us**, both under the 2000 us ceiling, and never summed; a single 2.4 ms refresh frame in the window exceeds the ceiling and makes the row `WARN` although the mean is unchanged.

### F-8. `hash_cost_per_s`

The `hash_cost_per_s` formula is defined as:

`hash_frame_spike_ms = t_hash_ms` on the one frame in every `DIAG_HASH_CADENCE_TICKS` ticks that runs the hash; `hash_cost_per_s = (TICK_RATE / DIAG_HASH_CADENCE_TICKS) x t_hash_ms`; amortised per tick `= t_hash_ms / DIAG_HASH_CADENCE_TICKS`; drift is caught within `DIAG_HASH_CADENCE_TICKS / TICK_RATE` seconds *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

**Worst-frame cost first, and an advisory illustration for the kernel amendment (DO-33b), not an Overlay gate** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: the hash is the kernel's cost and the Overlay never schedules or performs it. The amortised figures hide a probable one-frame spike: a `t_hash_ms` of 1.5 lands entirely on the frame that runs the hash, 37.5 percent of `STEP_BUDGET_MS` 4, while the average is 0.025 ms per tick. `t_hash_ms` is **unmeasured** (the 0.1 to about 5 range is an estimate): the cadence default is not accepted until spike S-6 measures the worst-frame cost (AC-DO-181). The formula is the kernel's to own; ownership of `hash_cost_per_s` moves to the Command & Simulation Layer (registry request) and this GDD keeps an advisory copy.

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `t_hash_ms` | float ms | 0.1 to about 5 (**unmeasured**; spike S-6) | Save/Load OQ-17 (sl:1316): a GDScript 64-bit hash over 100 to 300 KiB may exceed 1.5 ms | One `state_hash` (D.12, `H64`, SHA-256 first 8 bytes, ratified R-17); the worst-frame cost is this value on one frame *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass after design review)*
| `DIAG_HASH_CADENCE_TICKS` | int ticks | 6 to 600 `[PLACEHOLDER]`, a **kernel knob (accepted 2026-10-27, command-simulation-layer.md Tuning Knobs)**, default 60 | Tuning Knobs (kernel owner) | The kernel's hash cadence |
| `TICK_RATE` | int Hz | 60 | registry, cited | The kernel rate |

**Output Range:** a spike of `t_hash_ms` on one frame in every cadence, and 0 to about `TICK_RATE x t_hash_ms` ms per real second. **Example** with `t_hash_ms` 1.5 (a 1.5 ms spike on the hash frame at every cadence): cadence 1 (per tick, kern:915's wording) = 60 x 1.5 = **90 ms per second**, 9 percent of wall time, and each 1.5 ms spike would be 37.5 percent of `STEP_BUDGET_MS` 4 if it landed in P3; cadence 6 = 15 ms/s; cadence 60 (default) = **1.5 ms/s** (0.025 ms per tick), drift caught within 1 s; cadence 600 = 0.15 ms/s, drift caught within 10 s.

### F-9. `stage_entry_latency_s`

The `stage_entry_latency_s` formula is defined as:

`stage_entry_latency_ds = floor((T_enter - T_mark) x 10 / TICK_RATE)`, shown as `stage_entry_latency_s = stage_entry_latency_ds / 10`, computed **offline over the observer log** (DO-35): `T_mark` is the `T` of the `MARK` line with ordinal 1 and `T_enter` is the `T` of the first `SANITY_STAGE` line for the viewer with old 0 and new 1. The Overlay computes nothing of this in play and shows no verdict. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*

AC-SN-88 (sn:1626) requires reading the stage-1 entry window (268 to 363 s of boarding) off the Overlay: the Overlay supplies the stopwatch (the two log lines), Sanity supplies the window and owns the verdict.

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `T_mark` | int ticks | 0 to run length | The `MARK` log line with ordinal 1 (DO-31; the mark-ordinal table after DO-35), the tick of boarding | The stopwatch start |
| `T_enter` | int ticks | `T_mark` to run length | The first `SANITY_STAGE` log line (viewer, old 0, new 1), written at the class fold on which the model first reads stage 1: up to one fold (12 ticks, 0.2 s; 15 ticks on a catch-up frame) after the real change; *orchestrator ruling*: the Overlay subscribes to no Sanity change notification | The stage-1 entry |

**Output Range:** >= 0, in tenths of a second, when both lines exist and `T_enter >= T_mark`; no severity (Sanity's AC owns the 268 to 363 s window). Otherwise exactly one named outcome and no number: `NO_MARK` (no ordinal-1 `MARK` line), `NOT_OBSERVED` (no `SANITY_STAGE` 0-to-1 line for the viewer: stage 1 was never entered in the log) or `MARK_AFTER_ENTRY` (`T_mark > T_enter`: the observer marked late; a negative latency is never reported). The fold granularity (at most 0.25 s) is under 0.3 percent of the 95 s window. **Example:** `MARK` ordinal 1 at T = 600, `SANITY_STAGE` 0 to 1 line at T = 19,560 (a fold tick, a multiple of 12) -> floor(18,960 x 10 / 60) = 3160 tenths = **316.0 s**, inside 268 to 363 s (Sanity's own calibration hypothesis is 315.8 s, sn:344).

## Edge Cases

Format: *(E-DO-nn)* ids are cited by the rules, the open questions and the acceptance criteria. Hook ids in examples are the real ones only (`graph.propagate.power`, `graph.propagate.atmo`, `graph.propagate.failure`, `sanity.drift`). Every outcome is a display outcome: no edge case ever causes the Overlay to write, submit, force or repair anything.

**A. Kernel states and boundaries**

- **If the kernel fails to boot because a hook declares an unknown `after:` id or a cycle** *(E-DO-01)*: the kernel is `FAULTED` from `BOOTING` and never ticks; the Overlay (drawn despite no ticks, DO-11) shows the boot progress, the offending `hook_id` or the cycle path, and the FAULT record `(T, type, issuer, host_order, block_id, assertion)` with absent fields shown as `n/a`; every ref classifies `UNAVAILABLE` / `NOT_RUNNING`. The Overlay is the tool one reaches for when the kernel is least healthy (kern:606).
- **If the kernel enters `FAULTED` mid-run in a dev build** *(E-DO-02)*: `T` stays frozen, the FAULT record is pinned on `P-KERNEL` and on the strip, the other pages freeze on the last good views, page changes still work, and nothing is submitted. In `HIDDEN` the record is **not** drawn (a tester must not see it either, DO-11); `reveal` shows it on the strip. In a non-dev, non-playtest build the Overlay does not exist and the kernel returns to the menu; in the playtest preset the Overlay exists in `HIDDEN` and draws nothing, but the sink writes the `KERNEL_STATE` `FAULTED` line with all six record fields and flushes at once (DO-35), because a FAULT is the observer's most valuable datum *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*.
- **If the kernel is `PAUSED`** *(E-DO-03)*: `T` is frozen and numerals still refresh so a paused session is inspectable; the kernel's paused service cycle (CR-16: no P3, `T` unchanged) can still deliver P5 batches, which the Overlay counts in the dirty-key `last` figure but folds into no hook window; the hook window, the `n_eq` / `n_skew` counters and `t_ret` freeze (every counter advances only when `T` changed since the last fold, so a pause of 3 s that passes 180 rendered frames, or 600 frames, moves no grace and changes no class), so a long pause cannot accrue silent-lie time. A pause to read a defect therefore never creates one. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
- **If a restore, new world or transit completes while the Overlay is open** *(E-DO-04)*: on the `LOADING` to `RUNNING` edge every history window (P99 samples, peaks, silent-lie and skew counters, `RETURN` trackers) is cleared; for `DIAG_POST_RESTORE_SETTLE_TICKS` kernel ticks after the first `RUNNING` tick every ref classifies `UNAVAILABLE` / `SETTLING`, so the Filter's presentation-local tails (a return track dropped by the restore, E-PF-5) never read as `DEFECT_UNCAUSED`; the one `ALL` collapse that follows is shown `WARN` annotated `post_restore`; `hash_T` equals the restore tick (the forced restore hash, SL-12). Opening the Overlay mid-session starts empty windows and says "insufficient samples (n/N)" rather than issuing a verdict.
- **If the kernel is `SAVING`** *(E-DO-05)*: all pages stay live and the Save/Load channel's row shows `save_status` (`WRITING`, then `WRITTEN` with `written_at_T`); a `FAILED` status is a `DEFECT` row naming the last `SL_*` reason. The Overlay never delays or retries a save (display only).
- **If the Overlay's dev tag is absent or its root is missing from an export** *(E-DO-06)*: the composition root registers no `DIAGNOSTIC` subscription and no dev action, the Overlay root scene is never instantiated, and the CI file-list check fails the build if any `dev_artifact_list` path is present in a release export. There is no runtime branch to flip (DO-2), so a player cannot obtain the oracle by any setting.

**B. The classifier and the Sanity/Filter join**

- **If two `ACTIVE` ledger entries target one subject for the viewer** *(E-DO-07, PF-46)*: the ref keeps one row and one class, taken from the entry with the lowest `(start_run, lie_id)`, the Filter's own pick (PF-46, pf:467); Sanity now mints `lie_id` (sn:170, sn:345), so the Overlay's pick for `end_run` and `reach_age` is the lie drawn: the row shows the Filter's `lie_collisions` and the display-only `paired` mark. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* Two rows or two classes for one ref would let one entry's `DELIBERATE` hide the other's defect.
- **If the Filter's `lie_id` and Sanity's `slot` do not identify the same lie, or the Filter's `lie_id` is minted by a different rule than the ledger assumes** *(E-DO-08, OQ-DO-1)*: nothing changes, because the join is by `(viewer, subject_ref)` and never by `lie_id`; the Filter's `lie_id` is displayed as the Filter reports it and is used only for the `lie_id != 0` presence tests. A provenance change on either side cannot silently break the classifier.
- **If `lie_id` stays non-zero during `RETURN`, or is 0 throughout** *(E-DO-09, OQ-DO-2)*: the classes are unchanged. `SKEW` counts `lie_id != 0` only while `motion_mode != RETURN`; `DEFECT_NO_LEAK` excludes `RETURN` (the return amplitude fades to 0 at `LEAK_EPS_DEG`, so a dropped GLEAM is expected at the end); `RETURNING` tests `motion_mode == RETURN` and not `ledger_active` with no `lie_id` test. The Filter's confirmed behaviour (`lie_id` kept through `RETURN`, 0 once the follower closes; registry `filter_counters`) cannot flip a verdict.
- **If the Filter has not published `(value_lo, value_hi)` for a ref (`q_display` unknown)** *(E-DO-10, OQ-DO-2, OQ-DO-3)*: `step_enabled` is false for that ref, so the step test and the windowed progress floor are skipped and the return is held only by the stall bound `t_return_max(lie)`; if that is also unpublished the row shows "bound n/a" and a frozen or slow return is not flagged. (A `RETURN` exists only for a calibrated ref, PF-44, so this is a robustness path.) For the `tol` 0 kinds (`o2_pm`, `n_o2`, `n_inert`) with `q_display` unknown, `tol_eff` is 0 and the envelope phase is exact; a false `DEFECT_UNCAUSED` from one-per-mille rounding jitter is judged worse than a missed stuck return, so those refs are held by the time bound alone; `HONEST` stays exact equality either way. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
- **If a binary-frame ref (`SPRITE_SWAP`, `STATE_GLYPH`, `HOUSING_GLOW`, `MACHINE_FRAME`) is in `RETURN` with a non-zero `disc`** *(E-DO-11)*: the class is `DEFECT_UNCAUSED` at once. The frame cuts back to the honest frame at removal (pf:778); only the gleam decays over the Filter's 6.6 s, and a gleam is not a value discrepancy, so `disc` must already be 0.
- **If the first `WANDER_BLEND_S` seconds of an honest return move the displayed value by more than the follower's rate** *(E-DO-12)*: the envelope test (`disc_t <= disc_end + abs(tv_t - tv_end) + tol_eff`) holds, because it is anchored on the removal and not on the previous refresh; the step test starts only at `t_ret >= WANDER_BLEND_S` (cited from the Filter, 3 s). A per-step test across the fade-out would false-flag the wander term as a growing discrepancy.
- **If a return stalls, creeps, grows beyond the step or envelope bound, or outlasts the stall bound** *(E-DO-13)*: growth past the envelope or the step bound (slack `q_display`) makes the class `DEFECT_UNCAUSED` (`RETURN_GROWTH`) at the first fold it happens; a stalled follower (no fold in the window with `progress >= q_display`) fails the windowed floor within one `DIAG_RETURN_WINDOW_TICKS` window (`RETURN_STALLED`); a creeping follower that never re-anchors is caught when `t_stall` passes `t_return_max(lie)`. The bound measures time **without progress**, not total return time: `t_return_max` is per lie and scales with the latched `rm` (32 s at 1.0, 38.4 s at 0.6 for the 96-deg cap), and a long honest return on a degrading node (the true value receding faster than the follower closes, worked example 2) re-anchors the stall clock at every fold and is never a defect, so a reduced-motion player's honest 24 s return and an 80 s return on a falling node are both safe. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
- **If the Sanity row is `PENDING` (the 2 to 5 s onset delay), or has just left `ACTIVE` for `EXPIRED`, `DEBUNKED` or `INVALIDATED`** *(E-DO-14)*: a `PENDING` row is not `ledger_active` and no lie exists yet, so a true-equals-displayed ref reads `HONEST`, never a defect; an ended row is not active either, and the three end types are classified identically (`RETURNING`), because the Filter renders them identically (no relief cue, SN-16). The ledger row's `state` is shown beside the class so the operator still sees which end it was.
- **If a lie begins or ends between two numeral refreshes** *(E-DO-15)*: the per-frame marker updates in that frame; non-fold-fed numerals follow within `1 / DIAG_REFRESH_HZ` s; the class leaves `HONEST` within one class fold (12 ticks; 15 on a catch-up frame) and reaches `DELIBERATE` within two folds when the Filter picks the lie up on the next frame (at the 10 Hz rung; throttled rungs are AC-DO-148), and fold-fed numerals lag one fold plus one refresh; for at most `DIAG_SKEW_GRACE_TICKS` ticks the ledger and the `Perceived` record may disagree on presence, and that window reads `SKEW`; past `DIAG_SKEW_GRACE_TICKS` plus one fold the ref is `DEFECT_SILENT_LIE` at a lie's start (branch b: the Filter never picked the entry up) and `DEFECT_UNCAUSED` (`LIE_WITHOUT_LEDGER`) at a lie's end (the ledger row ended and the Filter's `lie_id` never cleared; AC-DO-195) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*. No fold shows an active lie with a stale `HONEST` tag for longer than that. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*
- **If a `GLIDE` lie departs slowly (any in-contract rate: 0.5 deg/s at the 40 s minimum life, lower at longer lives) so `disc` stays inside `tol_eff` for many seconds** *(E-DO-16)*: the class is `DELIBERATE` tagged `ONSET` until the lie age reaches `ceil(GLIDE_REACH_FRAC x (end_run - active_run))` runs; only then does `n_eq` run toward `DIAG_SILENT_LIE_GRACE_TICKS`, and only while the Filter's `lie_intent` says a visible departure was intended. A grace measured in frames alone would flag every honest slow onset, and a 24-frame grace at 144 fps was shorter than one Sanity run. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
- **If a repair makes the true value equal the lie's displayed target while the entry is still `ACTIVE`** *(E-DO-17)*: `disc` drops inside `tol_eff` for a moment; Sanity invalidates the lie at the next run when the true state equals the displayed state (sn:393, EC-SN-37), 12 ticks (one fold), which is inside the 120-tick grace, so no `DEFECT_SILENT_LIE` fires. Were the entry to persist beyond the grace, the defect would be correct: a lie that renders as the truth has no seam.
- **If a new lie starts on a ref that is in `RETURN`** *(E-DO-18)*: the Filter starts it from its own formula (pf:502); the ledger row is `ACTIVE` again, so the ref leaves `RETURNING` for `DELIBERATE` / `ONSET`, and `t_ret`, `n_eq` and `n_skew` reset when the earlier lie ended. Two lies never share a counter.
- **If the ref is a binary-frame surface** (`STATE_GLYPH`, `HOUSING_GLOW`, `MACHINE_FRAME`, or a `SPRITE_SWAP` lie) *(E-DO-19)*: `disc` is 0 or 1, `tol` is 0 and `q_display` is not applied; `DEFECT_SILENT_LIE` fires when the displayed state equals the true state past the age gate and the grace and the Filter's `lie_intent` has `not_drawn` NONE (a missing sheet frame, E-PF-12, is `DELIBERATE` tagged `NO_NEEDLE`). The age gate is `SWAP_LEAD_S` x 5 runs for every lie kind drawn on such a surface: `SPRITE_SWAP`, and a `FALSE_OK` or `FALSE_FAIL` drawn on a housing appearance frame (pf:82, PF-19); *orchestrator ruling*: they share the Filter's swap lead, so one age gate. A `FALSE_OK` or `FALSE_FAIL` on a needle or readout is not binary: it departs toward the zone at the per-lie rate (PF-13, D.3 `target_pm`), is a STALE readout (PF-18), and uses `reach_age` and `tol_eff`.

**C. Viewer, co-op and ownership hooks**

- **If the selected viewer is not the local seat** *(E-DO-20)*: the Overlay never calls `perceived` for it (the Filter answers the local seat only and returns the honest record for a remote `PlayerId` while counting `remote_queries`, PF-10); the ref classifies `UNAVAILABLE` / `REMOTE_VIEWER` and `P-TRUTH` reads "perceived: not queried". `remote_queries` therefore stays 0 in MVP and remains a pure leakage indicator.
- **If the build is a `MIRROR` (Vertical Slice B) and the viewer is its own seat** *(E-DO-21)*: the true/perceived pair is displayed, and the ref classifies `UNAVAILABLE` / `NO_LEDGER`, because `sanity.ledger` is `HOST_ONLY` and `ledger_active` cannot be evaluated; the pair is never judged `HONEST` or `DELIBERATE`. `HOST_MIRROR_MISMATCH` stays `n/a` (DO-34).
- **If the viewer is switched mid-lie** *(E-DO-22)*: every per-`(ref, viewer)` counter and tracker resets, and the next refresh reads `(tv, p, ledger)` for the new viewer only, so no row mixes two viewers; the viewer id is always on screen. Addressing is by `PlayerId`, never `peer_id`.
- **If the ref is `SCANNER`** *(E-DO-23)*: no true source is published, so the class is `UNAVAILABLE` / `NO_TRUE_SOURCE` (OQ-DO-2); it is never `HONEST` and never counted as clean in a page's severity roll-up.
- **If a ref has no registered resolver for the subject (`NO_RESOLVER`, which includes an unpublished or ambiguous `lie_targets(ref)`), or the render probe is absent** *(E-DO-24)*: the first is `UNAVAILABLE` and shows the reason (it also covers `ledger_listed` unpublished with an `ACTIVE` row on the subject, F-1 `scope`); the second leaves the `perceived` / `true_value` / ledger classes untouched and reads `bypass_check` = `ABSENT`, which is never shown as clean and never promoted to `UNAVAILABLE` on its own (DO-22).
- **If `tv` and `perceived` would straddle a tick boundary** *(E-DO-25)*: they cannot, because one refresh reads `tv` first, then `perceived` (a hit on the Filter's per-`(ref, viewer, frame)` memo), then the ledger row and counters, before any other callback (DO-20); a read split across a tick would otherwise show a false `DEFECT_UNCAUSED` on every tick boundary. Until the Filter states its memo fill order and process priority (OQ-DO-28), a `NO_LEDGER_DIFF` must persist for `DIAG_NO_LEDGER_DIFF_FOLDS` (2) consecutive folds before it is a defect, because the Overlay's call on a fold frame may be the first evaluation and not a memo hit *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*.
- **If more refs qualify than `DIAG_TRUTH_REFS_MAX`** *(E-DO-26)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: only the `P-TRUTH` rows are capped: refs with `ledger_active` rank first, then refs the Filter was asked about, then pinned refs; the remainder is dropped from the *displayed list* with a "+n not shown" line, and never from the classified set, whose `DEFECT` rows still count in the page header, the ring and the log. If the classified set itself exceeds `DIAG_FOLD_REFS_MAX`, the lowest-priority refs (pins first, then asked-about, never an Instruments ref) are not classified and the header, the strip and the `COVERAGE` log line show `N unclassified` as a `NOTE`; an unclassified ref is never shown as `HONEST`.
- **If a block's batch collapses to `ALL`** *(E-DO-27)*: `dirty_load_pm` reads 1000, `collapse_count` increases and the row is `WARN`, never `DEFECT` (an `ALL` collapse is legal kernel behaviour, kern D.9); after a restore the one collapse is annotated `post_restore` and the following silence is expected. A collapse that appears only because a catch-up frame merged several ticks' keys into one batch (kernel R-12, ratified 2026-10-27) is counted the same way and stays `WARN`: the union may exceed `MAX_DIRTY_KEYS` where no single tick did, and the Overlay neither splits the batch nor flags the kernel *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*
- **If a one-tick spike (a 256-key batch, a 5 ms hook, a `QUEUE_FULL` reject) lands between two numeral refreshes** *(E-DO-28)*: it is peak-held, so the refresh shows the maximum over the 6 folded ticks (at 10 Hz; a catch-up frame contributes its one merged batch, stamped with its last tick, *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*) and the event cannot fall between samples; a row that clears itself without anyone reading it would hide the very event the page exists to catch.
- **If fewer than `DIAG_P99_MIN_SAMPLES` Graph hook-tick samples are in the window** *(E-DO-29)*: `hook_window_p99` shows `insufficient samples (n/N)`, never a number and never `OK`; the window advances per Graph hook tick, so 99 samples need 3.3 s of `RUNNING` at 30 hook ticks per second (three per 6-tick step) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*.
- **If `DIAG_REFRESH_HZ` does not divide 60, or the rendered frame rate falls below it** *(E-DO-30)*: the model folds whatever ticks arrived (8.57 per refresh at 7 Hz; 7.5 per frame at 8 fps with a 10 Hz setting) and peak-hold keeps the figures honest; the effective refresh is the frame rate.
- **If `render_cpu_us` has fewer samples than `DIAG_FILTER_CPU_WINDOW_FRAMES`** *(E-DO-31)*: the median is shown as `insufficient samples (n/N)` with no verdict; once full it is compared with the registry's `CPU_BUDGET_MS` 0.5, a **median** per frame (AC-PF-43b), never per frame, so a single hitch is not a `WARN`.
- **If the kernel does not publish a hash cadence or a `hash_algo` field** *(E-DO-32, OQ-DO-5)*: the algorithm cell reads `H64 (algo untagged; SHA-256/8)` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings, consistency follow-up)*, the age of `state_hash` is shown with no severity, and a hash from a loaded save is compared only if both carry the same algorithm tag; with a tag mismatch (once the field exists) the compare is refused with `NOTE` "algo differs", never `HASH_DRIFT`.
- **If K-1 (the kernel's per-block cached hash) is absent, or an owner publishes no `block_hash()`** *(E-DO-33)*: the per-block column reads `n/a` (`NOTE`, not `DEFECT`) and `HASH_DRIFT` names no block; the Overlay never hashes a block itself, on a timer or on demand.
- **If a hook-window figure exceeds `GRAPH_HOOK_BUDGET_MS` on a dev build** *(E-DO-34)*: the row is `WARN`, labelled "dev template: indicative", and nothing more. The budget is defined on the Release export template where the Overlay is absent; the verdict belongs to the Graph's S1 spike and performance gate. The same holds for any capture taken with the Overlay `FULL`: its own cost (`overlay_cpu_us`) is in the number.
- **If the Overlay's own cost exceeds `OVERLAY_CPU_BUDGET_US` (window mean) or any frame exceeds `OVERLAY_FRAME_CEILING_US`** *(E-DO-35)*: the self-cost row is `WARN` (with the spike count), and the view throttles its **numeral refresh and drawing** along the data-list ladder `DIAG_REFRESH_LADDER_HZ` (default {10, 5, 2}; strictly decreasing, first entry `DIAG_REFRESH_HZ`, last at least 2): one rung down at the end of each `DIAG_SELF_COST_WINDOW_FRAMES` window that breaches, and one rung up (capped at `DIAG_REFRESH_HZ`) after `W_up` consecutive clean windows, `W_up` backing off 2, 4, 8 after a step-up that is followed by a breach (F-7) `[PLACEHOLDER]` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*, so a transient spike does not throttle the session for good and a persistent one does not limit-cycle. The ladder acts on the numeral refresh, which now includes the view update, the dominant term; at the last rung the row stays `WARN` and the budget is the technical director's (OQ-DO-13). Collection and the class fold are never skipped or slowed, so a throttled Overlay still sees every tick and **classifies identically** (F-6): the Overlay's own load cannot change its verdicts. `HIDDEN` collects only the observer set (DO-35), so `HIDDEN` and `FULL` are equivalent in every verdict the observer set carries, not in everything `FULL` shows. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
- **If a `dump` is written while the kernel is `PAUSED`** *(E-DO-36)*: the file is a pure function of the model, so two dumps at the same `T` are byte-identical; if `user://debug/` is unwritable the action shows a `NOTE` and the Overlay continues; beyond `DIAG_DUMP_MAX_FILES` the oldest dump is deleted, never an evidence file under `production/qa/evidence/`.

**E. Channels, presentation and use**

- **If a channel's `pull()` returns an invalid result, a registration carries an invalid `Callable`, or a page render receives invalid data** *(E-DO-37)*: one `DEFECT` row names the `channel_id` and the `DIAG_ERR` value (`NULL_RESULT`, `NOT_A_DICTIONARY`, `SCHEMA_MISMATCH`, `LIMIT_EXCEEDED`, `INVALID_CALLABLE`, `INCOMPLETE`), the other channels and pages are unaffected, and after `DIAG_PANEL_ERR_LATCH_N` consecutive failures the channel is `SUSPENDED` until the next mode change; the kernel stays `RUNNING` and the tick's `state_hash` is unchanged. A real script error inside a `pull()` is printed by the engine, aborts that function (which returns null, so the validator reports `NULL_RESULT`) and, under `-d`, stops the debugger: that belongs to the debugger and to CI (CR-19(c)), not to the Overlay. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
- **If a channel registers late, never registers, or collides** *(E-DO-38)*: channel registration is accepted only while the kernel is `BOOTING`, a rule this GDD adopts by analogy with Kernel CR-6, which itself covers blocks, command types and hooks only, so a channel that misses it never appears. The expected-channel list is a **data file** (`expected_channels`, kept with the composition root's dev registration code, *orchestrator ruling*; 14 entries by default: Horror, Body, Lighting, Emitter, Layout, Save/Load, Interaction, Controller, Web Map, Audio, Assembly, Suit, In-World Instruments, HUD & Suit Layer; Boarding is absent, Resolved 2026-10-27 — user ruling (design-review decisions): D98, OQ-DO-24; reversal would be one list entry) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*, and every count in this GDD and every criterion reads that list, never a literal. Graph (true values on P-TRUTH, hook timings on P-HOOKS, `graph_*` counters and `block_hash()` on P-STATE), Perception Filter, Pixel-Perfect Render Pipeline, Sanity, Attention and Chart Table are read through dedicated pages or views, not registered channels. An expected channel that is absent shows `NOTE absent`; an expected channel not yet registered during `BOOTING` shows `PENDING`; a duplicate `channel_id`, an unknown row kind, a unit embedded in a row name or a severity word other than `OK`, `NOTE`, `WARN`, `DEFECT` is `REJECTED` with an error row, never a crash. Exceeding `DIAG_CHANNELS_MAX` or `DIAG_ROWS_PER_CHANNEL_MAX` is the same. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
- **If a world debug draw uses a line width under 1 base pixel** *(E-DO-39)*: under 4.7 a line has no feather and vanishes, so every `draw_line` in the dev node uses at least `OVERLAY_DEBUG_DRAW_WIDTH_PX` 1 (pipe:923, `[REF breaking-changes.md]`).
- **If the layer-0 debug node is hidden by darkness, jolted by the camera or hue-graded at stage 3** *(E-DO-40, spike S-4)*: the draws are set unshaded in MVP and the spike measures their colours unchanged under `force_stage` 3; on failure the fallback is the layer-4 projection (OQ-DO-7), a Pipeline amendment request. The Overlay's world marks must not become corruptible by the lie they expose.
- **If the Attention phase is `CALM` and the TellAudit record is older than the last `tell_epoch`** *(E-DO-41)*: the TellAudit cell reads "last audit at T=..." and is `NOTE`, so a stale result is never read as current; any violation count above 0 is a `DEFECT` whatever the phase.
- **If the Overlay is `HIDDEN` and a plain action (`toggle`, `mode`, `page_next`, `page_prev`, `dump`, `cursor_next`, `cursor_prev`, `activate`) arrives** *(E-DO-42)*: it is ignored, so a tester who presses F3 reveals nothing; only the `reveal` chord (dev preset) and `mark` act. The chord leads to the strip, never to `FULL`; `mark` is silent (nothing is drawn) and writes a `MARK` line. In the playtest preset only `mark` exists at all, so no input leaves `HIDDEN` *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling R16)*. `HIDDEN` stays hidden even on `FAULTED`. The residual risk is a process risk the code cannot remove: a tester's screen, recording or stream that shows `FULL` or the strip voids that session's data (X-BS-11, AC-BS-122); a stray `mark` adds a spurious log line but cannot remove the real one (DO-35). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
- **If a designer or QA runs the Overlay `FULL` during a balance or perception playtest to "see the truth" and acts on it** *(E-DO-43, degenerate use)*: the session's findings about discoverability are void by the same oracle rule; the Overlay never gains a player-facing twin, and the fix for a lie that is only distinguishable with the Overlay open is a missing leak in the world (the design test), never a feature in the tool.
- **If the world's kernel `state_hash` differs with the Overlay on versus off over the same scripted run** *(E-DO-44)*: the Overlay is not shippable as a QA instrument; the replay acceptance criteria fail, the Overlay is excluded from the comparison harness until the leak (an allocation, a second `perceived` call that missed the memo, a retained block reference) is found. Inertness is the first acceptance theme.
- **If the player presses a key that matches a dev action in a release build** *(E-DO-45)*: nothing happens: the actions are not in the action map (DO-8) and the Overlay root is not in the export, so there is no hidden path and no accessibility or rebinding entry to find.
- **If a 4.6-style dual-focus change gives the Overlay keyboard focus** *(E-DO-46)*: non-interactive panels use `MOUSE_FILTER_IGNORE` and the Overlay never takes gameplay focus (the 4.6 dual-focus and 4.5 recursive mouse-filter behaviour `[REF modules/ui.md]`), so a click through the Overlay area reaches the game and gameplay input is never consumed by a diagnostic page.
- **If the Overlay is open across a save and restore of the world** *(E-DO-47)*: its mode, page, viewer, marks and windows are dev-session state and are not in the snapshot or `state_hash` (DO-9); after the restore the mode and page persist for the session, every history is cleared (the defect ring, the marks and the log are kept, DO-35), and nothing the Overlay holds can be restored into the world.
- **If `SceneTree.paused` is set by a menu or the pause command** *(E-DO-48)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: the Overlay root and its model driver run `PROCESS_MODE_ALWAYS` (DO-5), so the numerals keep refreshing and a paused game stays inspectable; the class fold does not run (no change of `run`), no counter moves, and no class changes.
- **If the Filter has not published `lie_intent` or `lie_targets(ref)`, or publishes them for some refs only** *(E-DO-49)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: the one degradation rule of F-1 (`scope`, DO-21 (5)(iii)) applies and nothing else. No `lie_intent`: row 4 is suspended and the ref reads `DELIBERATE` tagged `UNVERIFIED` (why `INTENT_UNKNOWN`), never a defect and never `LIE`. No `lie_targets(ref)` on a subject with more than one ref: exactly one live row (`ACTIVE` and listed) reads `DELIBERATE` tagged `UNVERIFIED` (why `SUBJECT_SCOPE`) for every ref of the subject, none asserted `HONEST`; zero live rows is `RESOLVED` (vacuous: every ref of the subject is not `ledger_active`, so the end-of-lie gap reads `SKEW`, never row 9, inside `DIAG_SKEW_GRACE_TICKS`); `ledger_listed` unpublished with an `ACTIVE` row reads `UNAVAILABLE` / `NO_RESOLVER` *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*; two or more live rows read `UNAVAILABLE` / `NO_RESOLVER`. The classifier gains silent-lie detection only as the Filter publishes (OQ-DO-26), the Overlay never guesses an intent from the displayed value, and the unverified count and the coverage readout (DO-21 (5)(v)) show how much of the set is unchecked.
- **If a stray `toggle` arrives in `FULL` or the strip** *(E-DO-50)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: it raises the confirm prompt and changes nothing; the prompt clears on any other action or after `DIAG_OFF_CONFIRM_S` of accumulated process delta; history is intact. Only a second `toggle` inside the window goes to `OFF`.
- **If the observer log cannot be created or written (disk full, unwritable `user://debug/`)** *(E-DO-51)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: the sink drops to the in-memory defect ring only, shows a `NOTE` in the header in `FULL`, echoes the failure to stdout when `OVERLAY_LOG_STDOUT` is on, and keeps running; the playtest preset writes a `LAUNCH` line first, so the observer can confirm the log is alive before the session starts.
- **If a release export is booted with the dev root absent** *(E-DO-52)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: `ResourceLoader.exists()` on the dev-root path is false, the composition root loads nothing, registers no subscription and no action, logs nothing above INFO, and boot succeeds (AC-DO-171).
- **If the seat leaves the compartment of a live lie** *(E-DO-53)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*: the ledger row stays `ACTIVE` and keeps its clock, `ledger_listed` stays true and the entry carries `away` true (Sanity SN-23, EC-SN-35, AC-SN-180); the Filter keeps its entry and plays no return on leaving or on coming back. The row is live exactly as when the seat stands beside it, so the class does not change: no `SKEW`, no `RETURNING`, no defect, on leaving or on coming back, and an `end_run` that passes while the seat is away ends the lie through the normal end-of-lie path (F-1 `scope`, vacuous case; AC-DO-195). The Overlay reads no `away` flag and has no walk-out state. Cites F-1 `live_row`, AC-DO-194.

## Dependencies

Dependencies are bidirectional: for each row the "Reverse mention" column says whether the other GDD already names the Overlay (checked by search at authoring), so a missing mention is a stated request, not a silent gap. "Direction" reads from this GDD's side. The Overlay depends on every system it reads (a design-time dependency on the owner's published read view or channel, never a runtime call into the owner's logic); nothing depends on the Overlay at runtime, and nothing in a release build can, because the Overlay is not there (DO-2). The index lists three dependencies for this system (idx:98: Command & Simulation Layer, Perception Filter, Pixel-Perfect Render Pipeline & Camera); the remaining rows are reads-from relations the index leaves implicit under its "depends on everything" note for the Polish layer (idx:207). **Freshness** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: re-verified against the current neighbour files on 2026-10-27 (see Interactions, Freshness); rows below are flipped where the neighbour amended itself. **Status vocabulary:** `designed` = locked in an earlier committed GDD; `designed (this run)` = on disk in the current orchestrated run, so its names are the owner's but may still move; `undesigned-provisional` = no file, the contract here is a request.
